 🔴4 Best Price Action setups


 Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  Friends, many times it happens that when we are analyzing the chart, we see that when a key level is formed on the chart and the price comes to this key level and breaks it, then we wait here that when the price makes a pullback then we will enter the buying trade.
  But after the breakout, the price moves up without any pullback and we keep waiting for the pullback. Here the main reason for missing the entry after the breakout is that you do not know how to understand the psychology of the candles after the breakout correctly. So in today's episode I am going to give you a powerful technique to identify real breakouts.
   Along  with this, we are also going to understand four powerful price action setups which work very well in the market. So if you want to avoid the trap, then watch the video carefully till the end. So let's start the video.  Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.
 Our first price action setup is the breakout rejection trap model.  In this, we see that when a key level is formed on the chart from where the price has already been rejected multiple times, then it means that the market is considering that level important.  At such a level, many sellers take sell trades and most of them place their stop loss above this level.
   For this reason, stop loss liquidity accumulates above this level. Now suppose the price reverses from the bottom and comes up and also breaks out of this level.  At this point, many traders buy after seeing the breakout. But we don't want to take a quick trade here. We have to carefully observe the candles formed after the breakout.
   If after the breakout two or more candles are formed whose upper wicks are very long, then this is an important signal.  This means that bears are repeatedly trying to push the price up. But sellers are stopping them every time and pushing the price down.  This means that even after the breakout, sellers are maintaining their control.
  In this situation we should just wait.  As soon as a strong bearish candle closes below this level, that becomes our confirmation. After this we can enter the short trade.  Stop loss will be placed above the high of that bearish candle and the target can be kept at the next key level.  Because in this condition the probability of a bearish move increases significantly.
   But always keep one thing in mind.  This setup will be considered stronger only when the upper wicks of the rejection candles formed after the breakout are  becoming longer than the upper wick of the previous candle.  This indicates that sellers are aggressively rejecting the price on every new push, which makes this setup even more powerful.
  Now let's understand the next setup.  Our second price action setup is the pin bar liquidity trap model.  In this we see that when a key level is formed on the chart and the price reaches this key level and breaks it. After that, a formation of multiple long upper week candles like this starts forming.  In which the upper week of each bearish pin bar candle is  becoming smaller in comparison to the upper week of its previous candle.
   This means that at this time big players are attracting retailers by forming bearish candles. So that retailers can sell after seeing these bearish pin bar candles.  When multiple bearish pin bar candles form on the chart after the breakout, many retailers see them and enter short trades and place their stop loss above the high of these pin bar candles.
 Due to this, a lot of liquidity starts getting generated on the upside. In such a case, there is a high chance that the price will tap this broken key level and move bullish to hunt for liquidity above. Now in this situation we do not have to hurry.  If the price gives a bullish reversal signal after the formation of pin bar candles like bullish pin bar candle or bullish engulfing then we can enter a buy trade from here.
   Because in such conditions the probability of the price moving bullishly is quite high.  But always keep one thing in mind that this setup works only when in the multiple bearish pin bar candles formed after the breakout, the upper wick of each pin bar candle is smaller than the upper wick of the previous candle.
   Now let's understand the next setup.  Now our third price action setup is the inside bar breakout.  In this we see that when a key level is formed on the chart and the price reaches this key level, then here we often get confused whether the price will go up or down from here.  Also, it is not understood whether this breakout is real or fake.
  To understand this we should always wait for the next candle.  If after the breakout the next candle closes below this level then it means that it is a fake breakout and in such a condition the price may go down.  But if the candle after the breakout forms a small body candle which is completely formed inside the breakout candle then it is called an inside bar candle.
   This means that sellers tried to drive the price down but were unsuccessful.  That means there is indecisive sentiment in the market at this time. Where a fight is going on between buyers and sellers.  Now, if after this inside bar candle, a candle is formed which closes above the high of the inside bar candle, then it means that now the bears have established their control over the price and now the price can move bullish from here.
   So from here we can enter a buying trade. Because in this condition the probability of the price moving bullishly is quite high. But always keep one thing in mind that this setup works well only when the body and wick of the inside bar are completely above the 50% area of ​​the breakout candle. Now let's understand the next setup.
Our fourth price action setup is the Doji Move Reversal setup.  When a key level is formed on the chart from where the price has previously made a strong move down, that level is considered a strong key level. Now when the price comes back to that level and forms multiple Doji candles on the way, it means that the bears are weak in the market at this time and the sellers are still strong.
 In this condition we should not take the trade immediately. We need to wait for the price to tap the level and give a strong bearish reversal signal from there.  If a bearish pin bar, bearish engulfing or a strong bearish rejection candle forms after touching the key level, we can enter a short trade from here. Because in this condition the probability of bearish movement of the price is very high.
 And if this setup is formed during an already ongoing bearish trend, its win rate increases even more.  So I hope you have understood all the price action setups explained in this video very well.   And when red candles are formed in the market, you sell and here too you often get trapped.     Along with this, in this course we also learn many powerful techniques which most of the traders never understand.  For example, when the market is moving up with such a strong move, we consider the buyers to be strong and buy. But then why does the market suddenly reverse ? We take entry after seeing a pin bar candle at some level.
 Why does the price still go against us? We mark the order block on the chart. But why does the price sometimes reverse before touching it ?  🔴5 Powerful pin bar trading strategy 


 Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  Many times it happens that when you are analyzing the chart , you see such a pin bar candle forming on the chart.   As soon as you see this, you immediately enter the buying trade from here.
 But when you enter a buy trade from here,  instead of moving in the direction of our entry, the price moves in the direction against our entry and you get trapped here.  Now the main reason for you getting trapped here is that you do not know when to trade the pinbar candle and when not to trade it. This is why you get trapped repeatedly when trading pin bar candles.
 So in today's episode we are going to learn five powerful strategies to trade pin bar candles. Which you might not have heard of before.  If you watch this video carefully till the end, you will understand very well when and how to trade the pin bar candle and when not to trade it. So let's start the video.
   Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.  Our first pin bar setup is the pin bar after breakout.  In this setup, we see that when a key level is formed on the chart and the price reaches this key level and breaks out, then we should not immediately enter a buying trade from here.
 We should wait a bit because sometimes after breaking the price level it comes back down.  Now if the next candle formed on the chart after the breakout candle becomes a strong bullish pin bar, then it can be a good signal for us. Because whenever a bullish pin bar is formed after a breakout of a key level, it means that at the time of formation of the pin bar candle, sellers had tried to take the price down.
 But as soon as the price came close to the broken level, the bears made strong buying and pushed the price up again. This is why the lower wick of the candle became larger and it became a bullish pin bar.  This simply means that sellers were unable to drive the price down and buyers defeated them. So in such a case we can enter a buy trade after the close of the bullish pin bar candle.
 Because when buyers appear so strong, the  chances of the price going up further from here increases.  So now we have seen how the pin bar formed after the breakout shows the strength of the buyers.  Now we can see similar strength of buyers in the pullback of the market also. Let us understand this now.
  Our second setup is multiple pinbar candles on pullbacks. In this, we see that when the market is moving in an uptrend forming a bullish structure and during this the price gives a pullback, then if we see multiple pinbar candles forming within this pullback, then we should understand the strength of the buyers here because at this time the sellers are repeatedly trying to take the price down.
)But buyers push the price up again every time by buying from the bottom. This is why pin bar candles start forming again and again. [nasal sound] But here we do n't have to buy immediately just because we see multiple pin bars.  We have to wait for the price to reach some important support level.
   This setup becomes stronger if the price touches the support level and forms a strong bullish pinbar candle there. Because here we are seeing strong rejection from bears along with the support level.  In this condition, we can enter a buy trade after the close of the bullish pinbar. Because the chances of the price going up again from the support level are quite good.
 Multiple pin bars in a Guys pullback are bullish signals. But in an impulsive move, the same multiple pinbars can also create a trap.  Let us understand this now.  Our third pin bar setup is multiple pin bar candles on an impulsive move.  In this we see that the market is moving in an uptrend forming a bullish structure.
 And during this time the price starts giving a strong impulsive move after a pullback. Now, if we see multiple bullish pin bar candles forming within this impulsive move, then we should be a little cautious here.  Because many retail traders are buying after seeing these pin bar candles and keeping their stop loss below the low of these pin bar candles.
 Now when many traders have their stop loss below the same point, liquidity accumulates there and the market often pushes the price down to absorb this liquidity. Therefore, in such a setup we should not buy immediately. We should wait to see if there is a  reversal signal at the top after the impulsive move.
  If we see a shooting star or bearish engulfing candle at the top, then we can consider a short trade from here because in such a case the price can first take the liquidity created below and then give a bearish move even further down.  So we just saw that we should be cautious when multiple pinbars form within an impulsive move.
 But if we get the same pinbar during retracement at any support level then the situation becomes completely different. Let us understand this also.  Our fourth pin bar setup is the pin bar on retracement. In this setup, we have to see that the market is moving in an overall bullish trend.  The price is moving up making higher highs and higher lows.
 Now, in the midst of this bullish trend, after giving a strong move, the price starts giving a pullback or retracement. If this pullback occurs at a key support level and a strong bullish pinbar candle forms at this support level, it could be a signal of bears becoming active. Because the large lower wick of the pin bar candle indicates that sellers tried to push the price down.
 But buyers bought at the support level and pushed the price up again. So in such a case we can enter a buy trade after the close of the bullish pin bar candle. Because the market trend here is also bullish.  The price is also at the support level and a bullish pin bar has also formed from the support level.
   Therefore, the possibility of the price going up again from here can be quite good.  Support plus pin bar is considered a good setup.  But if liquidity is combined with this setup, then this setup becomes even more powerful.  Let us understand this now. Now our fifth and my favorite setup is the pin bar plus liquidity setup. In this, we see that when the price is moving bullish or bearish and during this time, at some important level, we see a strong pin bar candle forming on the chart and in the same direction, we also see multiple liquidity levels forming.
 So such a setup is considered a high quality setup and we can trade such a setup confidently. Because in this case the probability of price  moving in the same direction to hunt these liquidity levels can be quite high.  One important thing you always have to keep in mind is that whatever setup you use, be it a pin bar setup or any other setup.
 If such liquidity levels are formed in the direction of the same setup , then the quality of such setup and the possibility of price moving in that direction increases significantly.  So I hope you have  understood all the pinbar candle setups very well.  If you have not yet liked the video and subscribed to the channel, then please like the video and subscribe to the channel.

(60) 🔴Stoploss hunting strategy 

many times it happens that when we enter a trade somewhere, the price first hits our stop loss and then moves in our trade direction. This is not luck.  This is not a coincidence either.

(00:24) This is a deliberate process.  And today I am going to explain to you the entire process step by step on a chart, how it happens and who does it.  The most important thing is that after understanding this, how can you use this process for your benefit. This strategy is called the Liquidity Hunting Strategy, and once you learn to spot it, you will see it on every chart.  Then only one thing will come out of your mouth.
(00:48) Dude, this was always in front of me.  I just couldn't see it.  So you have to watch this video carefully till the end.  You will definitely get to learn something different and advanced. So let's start the video.  Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.
(01:17) Let us first understand in simple words what liquidity is. Liquidity simply means orders. Where there are more orders, there is more liquidity.  Now think what a retail trader does when he sees a resistance level ?  He sells there and places his stop loss just above that resistance level and thousands of traders do the same thing simultaneously.
(01:41)   So what accumulates above that resistance level?  Thousands of stop loss orders.  When thousands of people's stop loss orders accumulate at this resistance level, this is what we call liquidity. Wherever there is liquidity on the chart , the market moves there to hunt for that liquidity. Now let us understand  why institutional traders hunt liquidities? Institutions, i.e.
(02:03) , large banks, hedge funds, and market makers, need buyers when they want to take a large sell position in the market.  There must be someone who buys those shares or contracts from them and where do they find those buyers?  Whereas in the stop loss orders of retail traders, they are placed just above the resistance.  So what do institutions do?  They push the price slightly above the resistance.
(02:24) All the retail traders' stop losses are hit and the institutions get all the buyers right there for their large quantities. After this the price rapidly falls back down from there.  This is called liquidity hunting and this is the most important part of today's strategy.  Now let us understand how to identify liquidity.
(02:43)   Look, liquidity is always where most traders place their stop losses and these stop losses are at very clear levels.  Look at this chart, this is a clear visible resistance level which the price has tested multiple times. Look here, the price came here and was rejected. Then I came here and was rejected again. The more times this level is tested, the more stop losses are placed above it.
(03:08) Because any trader who sees this chart sells here and places his stop loss just above it. Where is liquidity available? Above the previous day's highs and below the lows. Above clear swing highs and below swing lows and on equal highs where the price has touched the exact same level two or three times. This is like an invitation to the market that there are a lot of orders there.
(03:33) Come and gab them.  Look at this chart, equal highs have been formed here.  The price touched the exact same level twice.  Now think about how many stop losses there will be on this. This is a large liquidity pool and whenever it is swept, there will be a big move.  So now I hope you have understood  where the liquidities are.
(03:57)   So in step one we just do this.  Open the chart, identify obvious resistance levels and keep in mind that there is liquidity here. This analysis is not difficult at all. The clearer the level, the more liquidity there is.  Now comes the most important part and this is where most traders make mistakes.
(04:16)   When the price hits that resistance liquidity level, do not take a foreign trade because two things can happen here. Liquidity run and liquidity sweep and both are completely different.  First, understand liquidity run.  Look at this chart.   There was a resistance level here.  The price went up.   The resistance was broken and a strong bullish candle closed above the resistance.
(04:36)   The body is large and the close is well above resistance.  This is a liquidity run.  This means that this could be a real breakout and the price really wants to go up. In such a situation, taking a sell trade here is very risky. Now look at the liquidity sweep.  Here the price went above the resistance but the candle did not close above the resistance or even if it closed slightly above, the next candle immediately came back below the resistance.
(05:01)    The wick is long and the body is small.  This is liquidity sweep.  Price only went up for a moment. Everyone missed their stop losses and immediately fell back.  Learn to recognize this difference because this is the biggest skill of this strategy.  There is another easy way to understand this. Ask yourself.
(05:20)   Does the price really want to break this level or did it just go there for a short while?  If the candle is strong and the breakout is confirmed, then it is a run.  If the rejection is sharp and the wick is large then it is a sweep.  And remember we trade only sweeps. Always avoid runs.  Now that the liquidity sweep is confirmed, let us come to the final step.  Entry.
(05:43)   There are two ways to take entry and you can use any one according to your trading style.   The first method is immediate entry.  Immediately after the sweep.  Look at this chart, the price went above the resistance but in the end this candle closed below this resistance level and formed a pin bar candle.
(06:01)   This means that smart money has gagged liquidity and the price is starting to fall back.  You can take your entry right at the close of this candle. In this you get a very big target because you enter early but there is some risk also.  Second method: Retest entry a little more comfortably.  Once the sweep is done and the price comes back down, do n't rush forex.  Wait a little.
(06:23)    The price often retests once and that retest is a very safe and low risk entry point for you.  Look at this chart, after the sweep the price retested and rejected from there.  This is an even cleaner trade.  Now let us understand where to place stop loss and target? Always place the stop loss just above the high of that sweep.
(06:44) Because if the price crosses above it again, it means it was not a sweep but a breakout and our trade has gone wrong.  The target is the next major support level, the previous low, or a minimum 1:3 risk to reward ratio, whichever comes first.  So I hope you have understood liquidity hunting very well. (60) 🔴5 Support & Resistance strategy 

(00:03) Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  Many times when we are doing chart analysis, we see that some resistance level is formed on the chart.  When the price reaches that resistance level and gives a reversal signal there, we enter a short trade.
(00:23) But instead of coming down, the price goes up further.  Our stop loss hits and traps us. This happens because you know how to identify support and resistance levels well, but you do not know when to use which support or resistance level and how. This is why  you often get trapped while trading on support and resistance.
(00:46)   So today 's episode is part two of our support and resistance trading series.  In this tutorial, we will  learn five more powerful techniques for trading support and resistance levels. And if you haven't seen Part One of Support and Resistance Trading yet, then do  watch it after this video because in that too we have learned many techniques of trading support and resistance.
(01:04) So let's start the video.  Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.  Our first technique for trading support and resistance is the Doji plus breakout.  In this we see that when a resistance level is formed on the chart and the price reaches that resistance level, sometimes a Doji candle is formed there.
(01:37) Doji candle indicates that there is a situation of uncertainty or balance between buyers and sellers in the market at that time. Now, if the candle formed after this Doji candle breaks the high of the Doji candle as well as breaks out of the resistance level , then it can be considered a confirmation signal of the strength of the buyers.
(01:54)    This  indicates that the price is likely to move further up. In such a setup, we can consider entering a buy trade as the probability of a bullish move from this point is  comparatively high.  Similarly, when a support level is formed on the chart and the price reaches that support level, sometimes a doji candle is formed there.
(02:18) Doji candle indicates that there is a situation of uncertainty or balance between buyers and sellers in the market at that time. Now, if the candle formed after this Doji candle breaks the low of the Doji candle as well as the support level , then it can be considered a confirmation signal of the strength of the sellers.
(02:34)   This  indicates that the price is likely to move further down. In such a setup, we can consider entering a sell trade as the probability of a bearish move from this point is  comparatively high.  Now let us understand the next technique. Our second technique for trading support and resistance is the Confluence Zone.
(02:56)   In this we see that when the market is moving in a bullish trend following the trend line, during this time the price makes a pullback and creates a resistance level.  When the price moves up again after the pullback and breaks out of this resistance level, then this resistance level gets converted into support. Now when the price makes another pullback and comes down and the trend line is also overlapping with this support level, then this area becomes a high confluence zone.
(03:22)   If the price also gives a bullish reversal signal at this point, then it is considered a strong buying setup. Buyers are more active in such confluence areas. Therefore, a buy trade can be entered from here as the probability of the price moving bullish from this point is quite high. Now let's understand the next technique.
(03:40)  Our third technique for trading support and resistance levels is trend- based support resistance.  In this we see that many times when the price is moving in a bullish trend, during this time the price makes a pullback and reaches the resistance level. There the price taps resistance and gives a reversal signal.
(04:00)   Seeing this signal, we enter a short trade thinking that now the price will move bearish. But see, sometimes instead of coming down the price goes up.  Because of this our stop loss gets hit and we get trapped.  The reason for this is that we have marked the resistance level correctly and the reversal signal at the resistance is also correct.
(04:23)   But the biggest mistake is that we are taking trades against the trend.  When the market is in a bullish trend, buyers dominate.  That means buyers are stronger.  That is why we should not focus on selling setups in a bullish trend because selling setups often fail in such markets. In a bullish trend, we should focus on buying setups.
(04:44) When the price pulls back, one should  wait for a bullish reversal signal at a strong support level and enter a buy trade. Because in a bullish trend, the chances of bullish setups being successful are quite high. Similarly, when the market is in a bearish trend, sellers dominate.  That means sellers are stronger.
(05:06)   That is why we should not focus on buying setups in a bearish trend because buying setups often fail in such markets. In a bearish trend, we should focus on selling setups. When the price gives a pullback, one should  wait for a bearish reversal signal at a strong resistance level and enter a sell trade.  Because in a bearish trend, bearish setups have a higher chance of being successful.
(05:28) Now let us understand the next technique.  Our fourth technique for trading support and resistance is the pin bar after breakout.  In this we see that when a resistance level is formed on the chart and the price reaches that resistance level, sometimes a candle breaks out of the resistance level. Now after this breakout candle, when the next candle starts forming, the price first comes down a little after opening.
(05:56)   But a  strong buying rejection is seen from the resistance level which is now acting as support.  Due to this buying pressure, the price moves up again and the candle takes the form of a bullish pin bar. This setup indicates that bears are showing their strength in the market and the breakout is getting support.
(06:17)   When such a bullish pin bar is seen after a breakout, it can be seen as a breakout confirmation. In such a setup we can consider entering a buy trade. Because from this point the probability of the price continuing its bullish move is quite high.  Now let us understand the next technique. Our fifth technique for trading support and resistance levels is the breakout with a pin bar candle.
(06:43)   In this we see that when a resistance level is formed on the chart and the price reaches that resistance level, sometimes a candle is formed which first comes down a little after opening. After this, a strong buying rejection is seen in that candle due to which the price goes up and the candle breaks out of the resistance level and  takes the form of a bullish pin bar.
(07:04)   This setup indicates that the bears are quite strong in the market and the breakout is getting good support. When a breakout is accompanied by a bullish pin bar, it can be considered a strong confirmation of the breakout.  In such a setup, we can consider entering a buy trade as the probability of the price continuing its bullish move from this point is quite high.
(07:25)   Nevertheless, proper risk management and stop loss should be used before taking a trade. So I hope you have  understood these techniques of trading support resistance very well. (60) 🔴4 powerful Market structure hacks 

 Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  There are many traders who see that when the market is moving up, forming a bullish structure, they mark an order block like this.  Then he waits for the price to pull back to this order block and tap it to give a reversal signal, then he enters a buying trade here.
  They believe that the price will move back in the direction of its trend from here.  But see, when many retailers buy after seeing the reversal signal on this order block, then the  price goes down from here instead of going up. Because of this, all the retailers who have purchased from this order block get trapped.
   Now here the retailers get confused and start thinking that we had  marked a perfect order block after understanding the market trend and the price had also given a reversal signal by tapping this order block. Despite this, why did the price go down and our stop loss was hit ?  If this happens to you too, then watch this video carefully because in this episode we are going to understand five powerful hacks of market structure, due to lack of knowledge of which retailers often get trapped.
  So let's start the video.  Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.  [Music] Our first secret of market structure is not every pullback is tradable. As we know that whenever the market is in trend, the price first makes an impulsive move.
   After that it pulls back and again makes an impulsive move in the direction of the trend.  Traders like you and me often wait for a pullback after an impulsive move. When the price pulls back and reaches a reaction zone and we get a reversal signal, we enter a buy trade from here. Thinking that now the price will go up from here. But sometimes this does not happen.
 Instead of going up, the price goes down further.  Our stop loss is hit and then moves in the direction of the trend.  This happens because we do not know which type of pullbacks to trade and which type of pullbacks to avoid. Now the question arises as to which pullbacks are worth trading and which are not.
   To understand this, it is first important to understand that the market does not always continue the trend by giving a simple and clean pullback.  A simple pullback means that after an impulsive move, the price moves down forming consecutive bearish candles and during the pullback, we also see imbalances between the candles. When this happens, it means that sellers are still very aggressive in the market and are trying to push the price down.
  Liquidity is also very low on such pullbacks. Therefore, even if we see a bullish reversal candlestick pattern after such a pullback, we should not buy in haste.  Because in such cases, many times the price collects liquidity first. Our stop loss hits and then moves higher.  On the other hand, if after an impulsive move, the price makes a pullback in which mixed red and green candles are visible or there are small pullbacks within the pullback and during this time no fresh imbalance is created or the imbalance that was created has already been
 mitigated.  So this means that there is a lot of liquidity available above this pullback and the market is slowly collecting orders.  Now if such a pullback taps any reaction zone and we get a bullish reversal signal there, then we can consider a buying trade from here. Because in such a setup, the probability of the price moving in the direction of the trend is quite high.
 We call this type of pullback a corrective pullback.  And according to the market structure, this is considered a healthy and real pullback which we can trade with more confidence.  Now our second type of market structure secret is valid and invalid break of structure.  As we know, when the market is in a bullish structure and the price breaks above its previous higher high and closes, then it is called a break of structure or BOS.
   This simply means that the bears are in control of the market and the price has the potential to move higher.  But this is where many traders make a big mistake. Suppose the market is in a bullish trend.  The price pulls back and traders buy from here.  The price then moves above the previous higher high, but the candle does not close above that level.
 Yet many retail traders consider this a valid BOS and think that the market will go higher now.  For this reason, they neither trail their stop loss nor book their profits. But after some time the market moves opposite to their expectations. Instead of continuing higher, the price starts moving down and traders who considered this move as a valid BOS get trapped.
 Now the question is why does this happen ?  The biggest reason for this is that most traders do not understand the difference between valid and invalid BOS.  See, when the market is in a bullish trend and the price breaks the previous higher high and gives a strong close above it, then we consider it a valid break of structure.
  Because in this situation, the bears have not only broken the level but have also sustained the price above it. This shows the strength of buyers and supports the possibility of the market moving further up.  But if the price only forms a wick above the previous higher high and the candle closes below that level, it is not considered a valid BOS.
   In this situation, the buyers tried to push the price up but the sellers rejected the move and did not allow the price to sustain above the level. Such moves are often  called liquidity sweeps or invalid breaks of structure.  This gives us a signal that the strength of the bears is decreasing and the market may start becoming weak.
 That is why if we are already in a buying trade then we should be alert. In such cases, it may be a wise decision to protect profits , trail stop losses or think about exiting the trade. Remember, not every break of a high is a break of structure.  Just looking at breakouts is not enough to understand market structure properly.
    It  is equally important to look at the close of the candle and price acceptance after the breakout. Now our third type of market structure secret is the reaction zone trap.  In this we see that when the market is moving in a trend and the price creates an order block or any other reaction zone, then we wait that when the price comes back to this reaction zone, then we will buy from here.  Now when the price retraces and reaches this order block and gives a reversal signal by tapping it, we enter the buying trade from here.
 But the market does not reverse from here and goes up, instead it comes down and hits our stop loss.  The main reason why our stop loss gets hit here is that we enter the buying trade without understanding the price behavior.  Because if we understand the price behavior here, then when the price went up after creating the order block, it went up with a very strong move and within this move we can also see fair value gaps being formed.
 This means that the bears were very strong in the market at that time. But see, when the price came back to this order block, it also came down with a strong move creating an imbalance. This means that now the sellers are strong in the market. That is why we should not trade such setups because the chances of such setups failing are very high.
 We should plan a trade from any reaction zone only when the price comes to this reaction zone with a weak move and we do not see any imbalance within this move. And even if any imbalance has arisen, it should have been mitigated.  When the price moves weakly towards a reaction zone, it means that sellers are currently in the market.
In such a case, if the price  gives a reversal signal by tapping the reaction zone, then we can enter a buying trade from here.  Because in such a scenario, the probability of the price moving bullish from here is quite high. Now our fourth type of market structure secret is the strong move trap.
  In this we see that many times when the market is moving in an uptrend forming a bullish structure, then  during this many times the price pulls back and starts going up, then the price goes up forming big candles but we do not see any imbalance in this move, so we do not consider it as a strong move, rather we consider it as a weak move, which means that at this time the buyers in the market have become weak and during this time,  very high liquidity is available below the loss of these candles.
 In this case, we should not focus on the buying setup but we should wait.  When the price gives a reversal signal, you can enter a short trade by targeting this liquidity area. Because in such a situation, the probability of the price moving bearish from here is very high.  So I hope you  understood this Market Structure Secrets Part One episode well.

(60) 🔴 Support & Resistance Secrets part 1📊‼️price action ‼️Pkr Trading - YouTube

Transcripts:
Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  Many times it happens that when we are doing chart analysis , we mark a resistance level on the chart like this.  After that we wait for the price to reach here and then we will enter the short trade from here.
Now see, after some time the price comes to this resistance level and taps it and gives a bearish reversal signal. Like this, bearish pin bar candle formation is done.  Now, looking at this bearish pin bar candle, we enter a short trade from here. Thinking that now the price is going to move bearish from here.
When we enter a short trade from here, the price does not go down from here but goes up and traps us by hitting our stop loss. Now we get confused here and start thinking that we marked the perfect resistance level here and when the price came here, it also gave a bearish reversal signal. Despite this, why did our stop loss get hit? If you also do not know why our setup failed at the resistance level, then it means that you do not have complete knowledge of support and resistance.
In today's episode, we are going to understand some very important and powerful secrets of support and resistance  which all traders must know. If you do not know about these secrets then you will keep getting trapped at the support and resistance levels again and again. So now you have to watch this episode carefully till the end and do not skip the video at all.
  So let's start the video.  Please like the video and if you are new to this PKR Trading channel then please subscribe to the channel.  [Music] Let's first briefly understand what support and resistance are. See, when the market is going down and reverses from a particular point and goes up, then the place from where the price reverses and goes up is called the support level.
Many buyers are active here. When the price reaches this level again, there is a high chance that buyers who are active at this support will buy again and push the price up. Therefore, we have to enter the long trade by looking for a buying setup at the support level. Because there are high chances of the price reversing from the support and moving up.
Similarly, when the market is going up and reverses from a certain point and comes down, then the place from where the price has reversed and come down is called the resistance level.  Where many sellers are active.  When the price reaches this level again, there is a high chance that the sellers who are active at this resistance will again sell and push the price down.
  Therefore, we have to  enter the short trade by looking for a selling setup at the resistance level. Because the chances of the price reversing down from the resistance are very high. But many times it happens that due to lack of proper knowledge, we make very small mistakes while using support or resistance.  Due to which we get trapped.
So let us now understand some of its important secrets. Which we must keep in mind while trading support and resistance. Our first secret to support and resistance is to think in zones, not lines. Many times, when many beginner traders are doing chart analysis, they see that at the place from where the price has reversed and come down, they  mark a resistance level and start waiting that when the price reaches the resistance level, then they will enter the short trade from here.
Now watch as the price makes a retracement and reaches this resistance level.  But the price reverses down before they can tap the resistance level marked by them and they miss the entry.  The main reason for these people missing the entry is that they have marked the resistance level with just one line.
  Whereas we never have to draw support and resistance levels with lines.  We always have to mark the support and resistance levels as a zone.  The   entire wig that is left above the point from where the price reversed and came down, we mark that entire wig as a zone.  Now when the price comes to this resistance zone and  gives a reversal signal by tapping this zone, we can enter a short trade from here.
Because from here the probability of price moving upside is high.  The  biggest advantage of marking the support and resistance levels as a zone is that it greatly reduces the chances of missing our entry. Now let's understand the next secret. Now our second secret to support and resistance is the multiple touch weakness rule.
Many beginner traders believe that the more times a support or resistance level has been touched, the stronger it is considered.  Whereas this is absolutely wrong.   In fact, the more times a support or resistance level is touched, the weaker it becomes. Because see, when a resistance level is formed on the chart, it means that there is a lot of liquidity available above this resistance level.
This is because many sellers who have sold from here place their stop loss above this resistance.  Now, if the price reverses back to this resistance level, then  even more liquidity is generated above this resistance. Because sellers who sell again from this resistance also place their stop loss above this resistance.
  The side of the market where there is more liquidity, the probability of the market moving in that direction is also higher. Therefore, the more times the price taps the resistance level, the more liquidity is generated above it. This increases the chances of the price moving higher to hunt the liquidity present above that resistance level.
 You should always remember that if the price has  reversed from a support or resistance level at least twice, then before entering a trade, we need to see if when the price reaches that resistance level for the third time, it  breaks the resistance level, sweeps away the liquidity above it and then comes back down and forms a bearish pin bar candle.
  If this happens, we can enter a short trade from there. Because in this case the  probability of the price moving bearishly from there is very high.  But keep in mind that if the price has tapped a resistance level at least twice, then you should not trade until the price sweeps that resistance level.  We should take a short trade only if the price sweeps the resistance level for the third time.
Otherwise we do not have to make any trade plan.  Now let's understand the next support and resistance secret. Now our third secret is support and resistance interchange.  In this we see that when a resistance level is formed on the chart, then we wait that when the price comes to this resistance level, then we will enter the short trade from here.
  Now see, the price comes to the resistance level but no bearish setup is formed here, rather it breaks out of this resistance level.  Now this resistance level starts working as a support level. In such a situation, we can wait for the price to retracement and return to this support level and tap it to give a reversal signal, then we can enter a buying trade from here.
Because in such a case the probability of the price moving bullishly is very high. Now let's understand the next secret. Now our fourth support and resistance secret is the strong move week reversal. In this, there are many retailers who see that when such a resistance level is formed on the chart and the price reaches this resistance level with a strong move.
Then, if it is tapped and a bearish reversal signal like a pin bar candle is formed, retailers immediately enter a short trade from there. They think the price will go down now. But when retailers enter here, the price does not go down but goes up.  As a result, all the retailers who entered into short trades from here get trapped.
  The  main reason why retailers get trapped here is that whenever the price   reaches a support or resistance level with such a strong move and creates an imbalance, it means that the bears are very strong in the market at that time. In such a case, even if the price gives a bearish reversal signal by tapping the resistance level,  we should not plan a sell trade because at that time the buyers are stronger in the market and the chances of our stop loss getting hit are very high.
Now let's understand the next secret. Now our fifth support and resistance secret is Weak Move, Strong Reversal.  In this we see that when such a resistance level is formed on the chart from where the price has come down with a strong move, then it means that the sellers are very strong in the market at that time.
Now, if the price reaches this resistance level with a weak move and we do not see any imbalances within this move, then it means that the buyers in the market are very weak at this time.  In such a case, if the price gives a reversal signal by tapping this resistance level, then we can enter a short trade from there.
Because in this situation the probability of the price moving bearish from there is quite high. So I hope you have  understood these five secrets of support and resistance well. [Music] Let's first understand what the zone flip model means.  The zone flip model is a price action concept in which when a supply or resistance zone is broken and later acts as demand or support, this process is called a zone flip.  The zone flip model is based on three steps.  Breakout, Retest and Refusal.
  As I have already told you guys.  When a resistance level is formed on the chart and the price breaks out of that level, then the same resistance level later starts acting as support.  Similarly, when a support level is formed on the chart and the price breaks it, then the same support level later starts acting as resistance.
On the same concept, when a supply zone breaks out, the same supply zone later acts as a demand zone. And when a demand zone breaks down, the same demand zone later becomes a supply zone.  In this way, when a level or zone changes its role, we call this process zone flip or change in polarity.
  Now let us understand how the zone flip model works. In the zone flip model, we see that when the price breaks out of a resistance level , this resistance level now acts as support. Now in the second step we see that the price retests this support level.  When the second step is also completed, we look at the third step to see if there is any refusal when the price retests this support level.
  That  means some reversal signal should be visible here.  From which we come to know that buyers are strong in the market. Similarly, when the price breaks out of a support level, this support level now starts acting as resistance.
Now in the second step we see that the price retests this resistance level. That means there should be a reversal signal here which tells us that the sellers are strong in the market.

(60) 🔴Real V/s Fake Order block 


Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading App channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  More than 90% of traders are told that the last bearish candle before a strong bullish move is considered an order block.  Now when these people are doing chart analysis, they see that wherever such a strong bullish move has come from, the last bearish candle formed before this bullish move , this candle is marked as the order block zone.
Because they think it's a real order block.  Now see, when the price retraces and comes down, the price reverses and goes up without tapping this order block and all the traders who had marked this candle as order block  miss the entry.  The main reason for these people missing the entry is that they have marked the order block on the wrong candle.
Because the real order block candle here is this candle. Why am I saying this?  You will understand this very well after watching this video till the end. Because in this episode we are going to understand six such secrets of the order block which no one knows about yet. So you have to watch the video carefully till the end and do not skip the video at all.
  Only then will you understand everything well.  So let's start the video.  Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel. [Music] [Music] Let's first understand what an order block is so that even the complete beginners watching this video will understand everything clearly.
  An order block in trading is a specific price zone. Where large institutions or smart money have placed large amounts of buy or sell orders.  In SMC and ICT trading, the order block is seen as the base area from where smart money entered the market.  After which the price moved very fast and aggressively.   To put it in simple words, if the price moves upwards from a certain point with a very strong move, then it is said that the last red candle before this strong bullish move is  considered an order block candle.  While this is not the case,
 we do not consider the last bearish candle before a strong bullish move as an order block. Rather, we consider the candle that formed the swing low here before this strong bullish move as the  order block. Whether the candle is red or green does not matter here.  But this order block is considered valid only when a fair value gap is formed along with this candle.
  Now to trade this, we see that when the price retraces and comes to this order block, it should come from a weak move and we should not see any imbalance within this move. When this happens, it means that the buyers are strong in the market at this time and the sellers are weak. Now if the price signals a reversal or a change of character on this order block, we can enter a long trade from here.
  In such a situation, the  chances of the price moving bullish from here are very high.  Similarly, if the price falls aggressively from a certain point, the  last green candle before this strong bearish move is said to be an order block candle. Whereas this does not always happen.  We do not consider the last bullish candle before a strong bearish move as a direct order block.
Rather, we consider the candle that formed the swing high here before this strong bearish move as the order block.  Whether the candle is red or green does not matter here. But this order block is considered valid only when a fair value gap is formed with this candle.  Now to trade this, we see that when the price retraces and comes to this order block, it should come from a weak move and we should not see any imbalance within this move.
When this happens, it means that the buyers are strong in the market at this time and the sellers are weak. Now if the price signals a reversal or a change of character on this order block, we can enter a long trade from here.  In such a situation, the  probability of the price moving bearishly from here is very high.
  But many times it happens that in the chart we consider a candle like this as an order block and wait for the price to reach this order block then we should enter.  Now when the price retraces back, it either fails this order block or does not reach the order block at all.  This happens because we have not marked a valid order block.
So let us now understand one by one how a real and fake order block occurs.  In our first type of order block, we see that when the price comes down and reverses from some point and goes up with a strong move and here the candle forming the swing low is a bearish candle and along with this bearish candle a fair value gap is also visible, then we consider it a valid order block which means that smart money is really active here.
  In this case, we wait until the price comes to this order block with a weak move without creating any imbalance and gives a signal of reversal here, then we can enter a buying trade from here. Because in this case the  probability of the price moving bullish from here is very high.  If we understand it in bearish format, then in this we write that when the price goes up and reverses from a point and falls down with a strong move and here the candle making the swing high is a bullish candle and along with this bullish candle a fair value
gap is also seen, then we consider it as a valid order block which means that smart money is really active here.  In this case, we wait until the price comes to this order block without creating any imbalance with a weak move and gives a reversal signal here, then we can enter a selling trade from here because in this case the  probability of the price moving bearishly from here is very high.
  Now let's understand the next type of order block. Now in our second type of order block, we see that when the market comes down and reverses from some point and goes up, then beginner traders mark the last red candle before this strong bullish move as an order block and start waiting for the price to come to this order block, so that they can enter the buying trade from here.
  Now see, when the price retraces and reaches this order block, the price reverses and goes up before tapping this order block. This happens because these candles which you have marked as order blocks are actually not order blocks because this candle has completely formed inside this candle.  That's why we call it an inside bar candle.
And whenever an inside bar candle is seen forming at the bottom, then we mark this mother candle as an order block. Because this candle is also making a swing low. In such a situation, we do not have to mark this inside bar candle as an order block. In such a situation, we have to wait until the price retraces to this real order block and taps it to give a reversal signal, then we can enter the buying trade from here.
Because in such a situation, the probability of the price moving bullish from here is very high. Now let us understand the next order block type.  In our third type of order block, we see that when the price comes down and reverses from some point and goes up, and here the candle which has formed a swing low, a fair value gap is also formed along with this candle, even then we do not consider this type of order block as a valid order block because if you look carefully, a fair value gap is also formed along with this candle
and this candle has also formed a swing low. But this candle that has formed, the lower wick of this candle has mitigated this fair value gap by tapping it.  Hence this order block has now become an invalid order block , which no longer has strength.   Even if the price has come on this type of order block and gives a reversal signal, we should not buy from here.
Because in this case the chances of this order block failing are very high.  When the price moves up and reverses from a point and comes down and the candle that has formed the swing high here also has a fair value gap, then also we do not consider this type of order block as a valid order block. Because if you look carefully, there is a fair value gap along with this candle and this candle has also made a swing high.
But the upper wick of this candle that has formed taps this fair value gap and mitigates it.  Therefore, this order block now becomes an invalid order block that no longer has strength.   Even if the price comes back to this type of order block and gives a reversal signal , we should not sell from here. Because in this case the chances of this order block failing are very high.
Now let's understand the next type of order block. Now in our fourth type of order block, we see that when the price first comes down and then reverses from some point and goes up, here also more than 90% of the traders mark this red candle as an order block. Because they see a fair value gap forming with this candle.
Now when the price comes down, the price reverses and goes up without tapping the order block marked by the retailers and the retailers miss the entry here because they have marked the order block of the wrong candle here because the candle that has formed here has completely engulfed this candle and this same candle is also forming a swing low here.
  So in this case we consider this candle as the order block. In this case, we wait until the price makes a week retracement and reaches this bullish engulfing candle and taps it to give a reversal signal, then we can enter a buy trade from here. Because in this case the probability of the price moving bullish from here is very high.
  If we understand this in bearish format, then when the price first moves upwards and reverses from some point and starts falling down, here also more than 90% of the traders mark this green candle as an order block. Because they see a fair value gap forming with this candle.  Now when the price retraces upwards again, the price reverses and falls down without tapping the order block marked by the retailers and the retailers miss the entry here because they have marked the order block of the wrong candle because the bearish engulfing candle that has formed is completely engulfing this green candle
and the same candle is also making a swing high here. In this case, we consider this bearish engulfing candle as the actual order block. Then we wait for the price to make a week retracement and come to this bearish engulfing candle and tap it to give a bearish reversal signal, then we can enter a sell trade from here.
Because in this case the probability of the price moving bearishly from here is quite high. Now let us understand the next order block type.  Now in our fifth type of order block, we see that when the price comes down and reverses from some point and goes up with a strong move forming big bullish candles, but we do not see any imbalance in this move, so in this case we do not mark any order block here and we do not call this entire move a strong move but a weak move which is done only to get FOMO entry from the retailers.
In such a situation, we should not mark any order block here, rather we have to wait at the top.  When the price gives a reversal signal by tapping any level, then we have to enter the short trade from here. Because in such a situation, the probability of the price moving bearishly from here is very high. (60) 🔴7 Most Powerful Liquidity Trading strategy 

Transcripts:
Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  Many times it happens that when you are analyzing the chart , you see that the price breaks out of a resistance level and on seeing the breakout you immediately enter a buy trade.
Once you buy on the breakout, the price reverses down and hits your stop loss. This is what we call trap trading.  The main reason for your trap here is that you are not able to identify the liquidity.  That's why the market traps you again and again.  So in today's episode we are going to understand this concept in detail that what is trap trading?  How to identify traps using the seven liquidity patterns ?  And how to protect yourself from getting trapped?  And how to trade traps?  So let's start the video.  You must like the video and
subscribe to the channel and if you want a complete playlist of ICT concepts then please write ICT in the comment.  Let us first understand what trap trading is.  Understand trap trading in a simple way.  So suppose there is a resistance level here and the price breaks out of this resistance level. Now, seeing the breakout, millions of retailers start buying here because they feel that the price will go up after the breakout.
When a lot of retailers enter into a buy trade here, the price reverses down and the stop loss of all the retailers who bought on the breakout gets hit. This is what we call false breakout or trap trading.  These traps are set by big players to trap small traders.  Now a question might be coming to your mind that who are these big players and why do they trap retailers?  Let us tell you that big players in the market are not ordinary people.
  Rather, these are foreign institutional investors, domestic institutional investors, big banks, mutual funds and hedge funds. Who have huge funds.  That's why they  trade with huge amounts of money.  Their orders are so large that they cannot be filled so easily.  That's why these people trap retailers. If we understand this with an example, suppose there is a bank which has to sell Rs 200 crore and the price is trading at this resistance level.
  You know very well that when the price reaches resistance, everyone is interested in selling. If this bank also wants to sell its ₹200 crore here, then its order of ₹200 crore will not be filled because everyone here will be selling. To sell, it is necessary to have buyers and to buy, it is necessary to have sellers.
  No transaction is complete without buyers and sellers.  The same thing applies in our real life also.  At resistance, more than 95% of people are interested in selling. Therefore, if a very large sell order is placed here, the price will immediately fall and the entire order will not be filled.  So, to fill such large orders smoothly, these banks first break out this resistance level.
Now as soon as the price breaks out of this resistance level , millions of retailers start buying after seeing the breakout.  When buying starts here, the bank now gets a lot of buyers here. Due to which banks place all their sell orders here and their sell orders worth Rs 200 crore get filled easily and the price falls from here.
  This is the reason why a lot of trapping is seen in the market on the breakout of support or resistance levels. Now here also you have to remember that big players like banks, mutual funds or hedge funds prefer to place buy orders only when upside liquidity is available and prefer to place sell orders when downside high liquidity is available.
  These people do this because if the liquidity is high in the direction in which they do buying or selling, then  their profit is also huge. If we understand this with an example, then now look at this chart where a resistance level is formed.  When the price is moving towards this resistance level, the price is moving up with multiple retracement.
Which means that many retailers would have bought from these swing losses and they would have placed their stop loss below the swing loss. Now see, the price has broken this resistance level.  Now tell me whether institutional traders would prefer buying or selling in this condition? In this condition, institutional traders will prefer to sell.
  The reason for this is that many stop losses are placed below the downside swing loss. If the big players start selling from here and the price goes down, the  stop losses placed below these swing losses will start getting hit and the retailers who were buying from here will all be trapped. Due to which big players will get even bigger profits.
  Therefore, retailers  look at liquidity before buying or selling.  If there is high liquidity available on the upside, then these people place buying orders and if there is liquidity available on the downside, then they  prefer to place selling orders.  Similarly, there are many liquidity patterns that big players create before trapping retailers.
If you learn to understand these patterns, you can avoid the traps of big players and  generate good returns by trading with them. In this video I am going to tell you seven liquidity patterns.  Now you will have to watch the video carefully only then you will be able to save yourself from getting trapped.
  Our first type of liquidity pattern is called the compression to expansion breakout pattern.  In the Compression to Expansion breakout pattern, we see that when the price creates a resistance level somewhere and after creating the resistance, if the price comes down with a weak movement in which we see the formation of mixed red green candles, then it means that the sellers in the market are very weak at this time.
In this situation, a lot of liquidity is created over this entire movement because many retailers have sold from the red candle after the green one.  Now we see that if the price reverses from a point and goes  up creating an imbalance with a strong move and breaks out this resistance level, then it means that it is a genuine breakout because here this weak move indicates that the sellers are weak and this strong move after the weak move indicates that the buyers are strong.
  In such a situation, we can wait until the price retests this support and shows a reversal signal, then we can enter a buying trade. Apart from retest, there are some other setups which we explained in the previous video.  You can also use them for entry.  Now let's understand the next pattern.  Our second liquidity pattern is the Expansion and Compression Trap pattern.
  In this, we see that when the price forms a resistance level somewhere and from there it makes a strong move and expands downwards creating an imbalance, then it means that at this time the sellers are very strong in the market.  Now after this, if the price reverses from any point and goes up with compres
sion i.e. weak move, in which we see multiple mixed red green candles being formed.  So this means that the market is currently bears week and there is very high liquidity available below this move as many retailers are buying from the green candles after the red ones.  Now, in such a situation, even if the price breaks out of the resistance level,  we should not trade such breakouts because in such a situation, the chances of this breakout failing are very high because this move is telling us that at this time the sellers are strong in the market because in this, along with the
strong move, imbalance is also visible and this move is telling that at this time the buyers are weak.  In such a situation, we have to wait that after the breakout, if the price forms any bearish reversal candlesticks like bearish pin bar or bearish engulfing, then we can enter into a short trade from here because in such a situation there is a very high chance that the price will go down to hunt these liquidities and in such a situation, big players are also placing their sell orders from here.
Now let's understand the next pattern.  Our third liquidity pattern is the lower wax liquidity trap pattern.  In this we see that when a level is formed on the chart and the price reaches this level with a strong move or a weak move and breaks out of it.  But during this time, if the lower wicks of the maximum candles are becoming long in comparison to their upper wicks, then it means that very high liquidity is available below these wicks.
  Even in such a situation, we should not trade this breakout because the chances of such a breakout failing are very high because the liquidity is low at this time. In such a situation, you have to wait until you  get a reversal candlestick signal after the breakout, from here you can enter the short trade.
  Even in such a situation, the chances of going bearish to hunt liquidities below the price are very high.  Because in such a situation, even big players prefer to place sell orders above the breakout. Now let's understand the next pattern.  Now our fourth liquidity pattern is the Shadow High Liquidity Pattern.  In this we see that when a strong level is formed somewhere on the chart and the price breaks this level strongly with imbalance, then it means that the bears are very strong in the market at this time.
  Now we have to see if after the breakout we see some such retest in which we see multiple long copper wicks being formed.  But none of the candles are breaking the high of the previous candle. So this means that there is very high liquidity available on these wicks which has not been hunted yet. Because during this time, lakhs of retailers who do not have knowledge of liquidity are  selling here after seeing the pin bar candles because after seeing the pin bar candles, they feel that the breakout is going to fail.  Whereas this is just
liquidity creation.  In such a situation, there is a high chance that when the price reaches this support, it will move up from here to hunt these liquidities. So in this condition, we see that when the price retests and comes to the support level and gives a reversal signal here, then we can enter into a buying trade from here because in such a situation the chances of the price moving bullish are very high.  Now let's understand the next pattern.
Now our fifth liquidity pattern is the Weak Breakout Liquidity Trap pattern.  In this, we see that when there is a strong level somewhere on the chart and the price reaches this level and breaks it out, but during this time, among all the candles being formed, the upper wicks of the maximum candles are becoming long.
So this means that the bears are not very strong in the market at this time. In such a situation, there is a very high chance that this breakout may fail because even in such a situation, high liquidity is being generated below the loss of these candles,  which the market tries to hunt. In such a situation, we have to see that after the breakout, if any reversal candlesticks are seen forming like bearish pin bar or bearish engulfing candlestick, then we can enter the short trade from here.
Now let's understand the next pattern. Our sixth chart pattern is the structural breakout liquidity trap pattern.  In this, we see that when a strong key level is formed on the chart and the price comes down from there with a strong move, then it means that this is a strong key level where many sellers are active.
  Now if the price reaches this key level and breaks out this key level. But when the price reaches this level, the price comes with multiple retracement.  So this means that at this time the buyers are very weak and there is very high liquidity available below these swing losses. Therefore, we should not trade such breakouts but should wait for them.
  When the price gives a reversal signal and comes back below this level, we can enter a short trade from here because in such a situation, the chances of the price moving bearishly from here to hunt these liquidities are very high. Now let's understand the next pattern. Our seventh liquidity pattern is the supply rejection breakout.
  In this we see that when a strong key level is formed on the chart.  From where the price has made a strong bearish move.  If there is a strong supply zone just above it and when the price moves up with a weak move and breaks out of this level, then even in such a situation there is a very high chance that this breakout will fail because there are many sellers active at this resistance level and there are many sellers active in this supply zone also who do not want to let the price go up.
   In such a situation, as soon as the price taps the supply zone , sellers sell and push the price down.  So we should not trade this type of breakout but should wait for it.  When the price shows a reversal signal by tapping the supply zone, we can enter a short trade from here.  Because even in such a situation, the chances of the price moving bearishly from here are very high.

Let us first understand liquidity, what is liquidity? In simple language, liquidity means orders lying in the market.   That is,   liquidity exists wherever traders have placed their buy, sell and stop loss orders.
  Liquidity is mostly concentrated in the places where retail traders place their stop losses.  And normally retail traders place their stop loss above the swing high and below the swing low.  For example, suppose a high is formed on the chart.  Now many traders think that the price will go down from here, so they sell and for safety they place their stop loss slightly above that high.
Now since many people have their stop loss above this high, a lot of orders get collected in this area. This is what we call liquidity.  The liquidity that exists above the swing high is  called buy-side liquidity.  Now the market often moves towards those areas where there are more orders. So many times you will see that the price breaks the high first.
  It hits everyone's stop loss and then starts moving in the opposite direction. Exactly like this, if a swing low is formed on the chart, then many traders think that the price will now go up from here, so they take a buy position and for safety they place their stop loss slightly below that swing low.  Now since many buyers have their stop loss below that low, a lot of sell orders get collected in that area.
This is what we call sell side liquidity.  The liquidity that exists below the swing low is what we call sell-side liquidity. So many times you will see the price go below the first swing low. Hits everyone's stop loss and starts moving up again after they collect their liquidity. So in simple words, remember that liquidity is where the traders have their stop losses.
Now let us understand why the market hunts these liquidities. See, the big players in the market i.e. smart money and institutions buy and sell in very large quantities. And to execute such large orders they need a lot of orders.  Which we call liquidity.  Now where is the maximum liquidity available in the market?   That's where retail traders place their stop losses.
  For example, sellers have stop losses above the swing high and buyers have stop losses below the swing low.  Therefore, the market often moves towards these areas. When the price breaks a swing high, the  stop losses of the sellers placed there start getting hit.  And as soon as the stop losses are hit, the market gets a lot of buy orders.

Exactly like this, when the price goes below a swing low, the stop losses of the buyers placed there start getting hit.

This allows their buy orders to get filled easily and the price starts moving upwards from there. This is why we often see a lot of breakdowns also fail.
Whenever the market takes a reversal by hunting liquidities at a swing high or swing low , we call it a liquidity sweep or liquidity hunt.  Now let us understand the fair value gap a little. After that we will also learn a powerful strategy to trade by combining liquidity sweep and fair value gap.  Before understanding the fair value gap, we need to understand imbalance.
See, normally the market moves step by step.  The price moves forward, trading slightly both up and down.  But sometimes there is suddenly very strong buying or strong selling in the market. Due to which the price moves very fast in one direction.  And when the market moves so fast, there are some price areas in between where proper trading cannot take place.
  That means the market does not trade in a balanced manner in those areas. This condition is called imbalance.  Now due to this imbalance a gap is created on the chart which we call Fair Value Gap or FVG. In simple language, fair value gap is the area where the market has moved very fast and the price has not traded properly there.
Whenever the price moves on the chart creating a fair value gap with imbalance, it means that the smart money in the market at that time is also trading in the same direction.  And in this case also we should trade in the direction of the imbalance move. But if we see movement somewhere on the chart in such a way that we see big candles formed in one direction.
But fair value gaps are not visible within this move , so we do not call it a strong move.  And in such a situation we should not trade in the direction of this move. Rather, in this case, we should look for a setup in the opposite direction of this move and trade.  Because in such a situation the price often reverses.

[Music] Let's first understand what a reclaimed block is and where it appears on the chart.   Because when the price breaks out of this level and goes up, the price goes up by forming multiple candles.  Whereas when the price is retesting, the entire move is covered in just a few candles. In this case, even if a reversal signal is received at this broken level,  we should not trade from here.

In which we do not see any imbalance.  This means that at this time in the market, sellers are very weak and buyers are strong. Whenever an order block is formed on the chart, it means that smart money has placed its heavy orders here.

That is why the price has come to this order block with a strong move in which imbalances remain. In this case, even if we get a reversal signal on the order block, we should not buy from here because at this time the selling pressure in the market has increased.  That's why these setups fail.
  But if there is an order block formed on the chart and the price comes on this order block with a weak move forming red green candles in such a way that we do not see any major imbalance, then it means that the sellers are weak in the market at this time.  Now if the price signals a reversal on this order block , we can confidently enter a buy trade from here.
Because in such a situation, the probability of the price reversing from here and moving bullish is very high.
