(60) 🔴Liquidity Is evening 

Transcripts:
Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading Channel.  Where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  There are many beginner traders.  When you are analyzing the chart, you see that when the price reaches a support level, you buy from here.  Because you have been told that buyers become active at support and push the price up.
But when you buy from here, the price first hits your stop loss and then the price moves in the direction of your entry and you get trapped.  Then you start wondering that when the market was supposed to go up, then  why does the market first come down and hit my stop loss and only then why does it go up. If you do not know why the market does this, then let me tell you that the main reason behind this is liquidity.
  You do not have proper knowledge of liquidity concept.  That's why you get trapped here.  In today's episode, we are going to  understand the liquidity concept in detail.  What is liquidity? How to identify liquidity? What are liquidity zones? What are liquidity pools?  And what is a liquidity sweep?  So the video is going to be very important.
So you have to watch the video carefully till the end.  Only then will you understand everything well. So let's start the video.  Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.  [Music] Let's first understand what liquidity is.  Let us understand this in a very simple way.
Suppose you have 1000 water bottles and you want to sell them. You go to a cricket stadium but there is no match going on there at that time.  There will hardly be 8 to 10 people in the entire stadium.  You wo n't find any buyers and it will take a long time to sell your bottles. This means that there are fewer buyers there.  That means it is a place with low liquidity.
Now you reach the same stadium on the day of the match with these 1000 water bottles.   There is crowd all around.  At this time the demand for water is very high.  So all your bottles will be sold in a matter of minutes.  This is a place with high liquidity where there is a lot of buyers.  The same thing happens in trading.
Where there are more buyers and sellers, liquidity is high and orders are filled easily.  Where there are fewer buyers or sellers, liquidity is low and orders are difficult to fill. This is the concept of liquidity in simple language.  To understand the market, it is first important to understand  why liquidity is so important in trading.
Before understanding liquidity, one thing should be clear that there are two types of people in the market. First are the retail traders who trade with small capital.  Like 10 lakh, 20 lakh or maximum 1 crore. This amount is very small from the market point of view. And in this category come normal traders like you and me.
   The second are the big players whom we also call smart money.  They have a huge fund.  That is why these people trade from Rs 50 crore to thousands of crores. These include banks, hedge funds, FIIs and DIIs.  Meaning, basically only two categories are active in the market. Retail Traders and Big Players.  Now understand carefully what I am explaining further.
Retail traders have smaller orders. Therefore their buying or selling becomes easy. But the orders of big players are so big that they are not filled easily.  This is why big players always look for liquidity.  A place where they can find buyers or sellers so that their heavy orders can be executed. Suppose the market is moving bullish.
  Then it gives a pullback and then breaks the resistance while continuing the trend.  Seeing this breakout, many retail traders start buying because they feel that the market will now go up. But if at this point a big player wants to buy goods worth Rs 100 crore, his orders will not be filled because there are many buyers here but no sellers.
  That means there is less liquidity in the market at this moment. Unless the market is liquidated, no major move will take place because liquidity is the fuel of the market. Now if a big player wants to make a purchase of Rs 100 crore, then obviously he will need sellers. What do big players do to bring in sellers ?  He places a large sell order in the market as buyers are already available there.
  As soon as the big player sells, the price comes down a bit and the support level gets broken. As soon as the support is broken, the stop losses of retail buyers are hit and their stop losses behave like sell orders. Seeing the breakdown, some retail traders think that the market is going into a downtrend, so they also start selling.
  At this point, big players get sellers.  First due to the stop loss being hit by the buyers and then due to the selling by the retail traders on the breakdown.  Big players fill their heavy buying orders here as soon as they find enough sellers.  As soon as the orders are filled, the price shoots up again.  In this way you can see how important liquidity is in the market.
Where there is liquidity, big players execute their big orders and that is where strong moves are made.  For example, look at this chart and see that the market is moving bullish here.   There is a support level here.  The price comes to this support level and as soon as a bullish candle forms here, many retail traders start buying and they place their stop loss below this support.
  So the place where the stop losses of these buyers are placed is called the liquidity zone.  Now watch as the price moves up and breaks out of this resistance. Due to which this resistance now starts acting as support.  Now see, when the price retraces, again as soon as we see a bullish candle at this support level, retail traders start buying here and they place their stop loss below this support.
Now the stop loss of these buyers is below this support, so this is what we call the liquidity zone. And the market always targets the liquidity zone.  Like now see the price and liquidity hunts and then goes up once again.  You always have to remember that wherever people place their stop losses, it acts as a liquidity zone and the market hunts for liquidity before making any sideways move.
After that he gives his real move. Now let's understand liquidity pools. Now look at this chart in which the price has moved like this earlier. After that it has moved sideways in a range. So a lot of people have stop losses above this range and below this range.  That's why we call it a liquidity pool. Because many sellers who are selling from here keep their stop loss higher.
And many buyers who have bought from here,  place their stop loss here.  That is why we call the zones above and below this range the liquidity pool. Now a question might be coming to your mind that are liquidity pool and liquidity zone the same?  So the answer is no.  Liquidity pools and liquidity zones are not the same.
Because a liquidity pool is created when a large number of people are buying or selling. Due to which more stop losses are also created. Whereas this does not happen at all in the liquidity zone.  We can call any zone where there is liquidity, that is, where people have stop losses.  Whether the stop loss is high or low.
  If we understand it in a simpler way, then we can say that when the price is rejected from a level multiple times, then a liquidity pool is created below or above that level. Now the third point where there is liquidity is swing highs and swing lows. Whatever swing highs and swing lows there are in the market, whether the swings are small or big, there is liquidity above those swing highs and below those swing lows.
Because at the swing high sellers are selling and at the swing low many buyers are buying.  Now there is the fourth point where there is liquidity.  Equal highs and equal lows.  Whenever at least two highs or lows remain equal in the market, they are called equal highs or equal lows.  There is  also a lot of liquidity available from buyers and sellers above equal highs and below equal lows.
   The  market targets these liquidities. But if you want to avoid getting liquidated i.e. trapped, then I am telling you some important points. By following which you can avoid getting liquidated.  The first point is that you should always wait for the candle closing before entering. Never enter without candle closing.
  For example, suppose the market is moving bearish here and the price breaks out of this resistance level. Now as soon as the price breaks out of this resistance , many buyers start buying. But when this candle closes, it becomes a rejection candle and after that the price goes down from here and all the people who had bought from here get trapped.
That is why we must wait for the candle close before entering because only the closing of the candle tells us the real logic of the chart.  The second point to avoid the liquidity trap is that we should  not trade only on the basis of small time frames.  Because it is very easy to manipulate the price on small time frames and big players can manipulate it easily.
  Therefore, before taking entry on small time frames, we should understand the market sentiment well on larger time frames because it is very difficult to manipulate the price on larger time frames. Now the third point to avoid the liquidity trap is that you should never trade in penny stocks. Because there is very little liquidity i.e. buyers and sellers in penny stocks.
  Penny stocks are very easy to manipulate and big players can easily move the price up and down.  Because of this, retail traders often enter FOMO. But when the time for exit comes, buyers are not found and the stock gets stuck in the lower circuit.  Technical analysis is also not reliable in penny stocks. Therefore, the capital risk is very high.
Therefore, to avoid liquidity trap, one should always trade in stocks that are high liquidity, fundamentally strong and have good volume. So I hope you have understood everything about liquidity well. (60) 🔴Ict Rejection block Strategy 

Transcripts:
Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  Friends, many times it happens that when we are looking at the chart and the price is moving in a particular direction, then the price suddenly faces a rejection from some point and forms a rejection candle here and just after this rejection candle the price comes down with a strong move.
  Now we get confused here as to what was there due to which the price fell from here.  If you don't know why the price fell from here then it means that you do not have knowledge of the rejection block concept of ICT.  So today's video is going to be part four of our ICT series. In which we are going to understand this topic in detail that what is rejection block?  When is a rejection block created?  What is the psychology of rejection block ?  And how do we trade the rejection block ?  So the video is going to be very
long.  Therefore, you have to  watch the video carefully till the end and do not skip the video at all. So let's start the video. Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.  [Music] Let's first understand what a rejection block is.  The concept of ICT rejection block is mainly based on rejection wicks and liquidity sweeps.
  When the market price targets the liquidity of previous highs or lows, a  strong reaction often follows.  This reaction creates a long rejection wick candle on the chart, indicating that the price has been rejected from there. Now, if after this rejection the market breaks its structure, then the area in which this strong rejection took place is called the rejection block.
  Let us  understand this in a more simple and practical way so that you can understand it better. See, when a level is formed on the chart and the price goes up and breaks that level, then at first it seems as if a breakout has occurred.  But often big players are active above that level who do not allow the price to sustain above that level.
  For this reason, big players do heavy selling from here and a strong bearish rejection is seen in this candle. Due to this rejection, this candle becomes a long upper week rejection candle which means that the big players do not want the price to go up. Now, if the price moves strongly immediately after this long upper wick rejection candle, then the upper wick of the candle which has formed the upper wick long is called the rejection block.
We consider this rejection block valid only when the open, high, low and close of the next candle formed after the formation of the rejection candle are all below the rejection block.  Also, a fair value gap should be formed along with the rejection block candle.  And when the price makes a strong bearish move after forming a rejection block, then during that time the price must also break some level.
  Only then do we consider it a valid rejection block.  When all these criteria are fulfilled, the proposition is considered valid and of high probability. Which means that really big players have been selling from this block. If the upper wick of the rejection block candle  is tapped by the wick of the following candle, then this rejection block is considered a weak rejection block.
Whose win rate decreases a bit.  When a valid rejection block is formed on the chart and the price retraces back to that rejection block and taps it to give a reversal signal, then we have to enter the short trade from here because in this case the probability of the price reversing from this rejection block and moving downwards is quite high.
Now let us understand this in a bullish context also. See, when a level is formed on the chart and the price goes down and breaks that level, then at first it seems as if a breakdown has occurred.  But often big players are active below that level who do not allow the price to sustain below that level.
  For this reason, big players do heavy buying from here and a strong bullish rejection is seen in this candle. Due to this rejection, this candle becomes a long lower weak rejection candle.  Which means that big players do not want to let the price go down. Now, if the price moves strongly immediately after this long lower wick rejection candle, then the  lower wick of the candle whose lower wick is long is called the rejection block.
We consider this rejection block valid only when the open, high, low and close of the next candle formed after the formation of the rejection candle are all above the rejection block. Also, a fair value gap should be formed along with the rejection block candle. And when the price makes a strong bullish move after forming a rejection block, then during that time the price must also break some level.
  Only then do we consider it a valid rejection block.  When all these criteria are fulfilled, the rejection is considered valid and of high probability. Which means that really big players have bought from this block. If the lower wick of a rejection block candle  is tapped by the wick of the following candle, then this rejection block is considered a weak rejection block.
  Whose win rate decreases a bit.  When a valid rejection block is formed on the chart and the price retraces back to that rejection block and taps it to give a reversal signal, then we have to enter a long trade from here because in this case the probability of the price reversing from this rejection block and moving upside is quite high.
  Now, one important point you have to remember is that the rejection block can be formed even without sweeping any level of liquidity. But all other criteria should be fulfilled.  Let us understand the rejection block with a real chart example.  For example, you can see on this chart that the price has moved like this and a swing high has been formed here above which high liquidity is available.
Now look, the price has gone up and has hunted the liquidity of this high and has formed a strong bearish rejection candle.  Which means that there is some big power which is not allowing the price to go above this level.  Now see as a long upper week rejection candle is formed here.  The price has moved down from here with a strong move and a fair value gap is also seen forming along with this long upper week rejection candle.
 And when the price made a strong bearish move from here, the price also broke down this level.  This means that this strong move has actually happened due to heavy selling by some big players. So now we have to mark the upper wick of this rejection candle whose upper wick is long as a zone which is a rejection block.
  Now see, when the price has come back to this rejection while retracing, we can see the formation of a pin bar candle here. This means that the price can now move bearish from here.  So now we can also enter a short trade from here. After that you can see that the price has moved down from here in a bearish move.
Now if we understand this in bullish format, then on this chart you can see that there is a level below which high liquidity is available.  Now see, when the price has come down, the price has swept the liquidity of this level and formed a strong bullish rejection candle whose lower week has remained very long.
  This means that there is some big power below this level which is not allowing the price to sustain below. Now see as a long lower weak rejection candle is formed here. Just after this, the price has made a bullish move with a strong move and a fair value gap is also formed along with this rejection candle and when the price has gone up from here with a strong move, the price has also broken out this level which means that this strong move has come due to heavy buying by big players.
So now we are marking the lower wick of this rejection candle which is long as a zone which is a rejection block.  Now see, when the price has come to this rejection block, as soon as the price has tapped it, a bullish candle has been formed here in which we are seeing buying pressure. This means that the price can now move bullish from here.
  So now we can also enter a long trade from here. After that you can see that the price has moved up from here in a bullish move.  So I hope you have understood everything about the rejection block very well. (60) 🔴High probability supply & demand Strategy 

Transcripts:
Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  Friends, many times it happens that when we are analyzing the chart, we mark a supply zone somewhere on the chart and wait that when the price comes to this supply zone , then we will enter the short trade from here.
Now see, when the price takes a retracement from some point and reaches this supply zone and gives a bearish reversal signal by tapping the supply zone, then we enter the short trade from here. Thinking that the price will now go down.  But see, when we enter a short trade from here,  our stop loss is hit before the price goes down and then it goes down in our expected direction.
Now we get confused here that why did the market hit our stop loss first and then why did it go down? Whereas we had marked a strong supply zone and entered the short trade after seeing a clear bearish pin bar candle at this supply zone.   Why was our stop loss still hit?  If this happens to you and you don't know the reason, it means that you don't know how to identify high probability supply demand zones and understand when to trade a supply or demand zone and when not to trade it.
  So in today's episode we are going to understand this thing in detail.  How to identify high probability supply demand zones and when to trade and when not to trade a supply or demand zone.  Along with this, at the  end of the video I am going to give you one of the strongest supply demand setups which has a very high win rate and this setup is my favorite setup which I also use myself.

Otherwise you will keep getting trapped again and again while trading the supply demand zones. So let's start the video.  Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.
 [Music] Let's first understand what a supply and demand zone is.  Supply and demand zones are very simple to understand.  When the price moves upwards from a particular area on the chart with strong buying pressure , we call that area a demand zone.  In simple language, the demand zone is the area from where the bears became aggressively active and the price got a strong bullish move.
  Similarly, when the price moves downwards from a particular area with strong selling pressure, then we call that area a supply zone.  That is, the supply zone is the area from where sellers became aggressively active and the price got a strong bearish move. Now, if the price on the chart again reaches any demand zone in the future, then there is a possibility of getting a bullish reaction from there.
And if the price comes back to any supply zone then there is a possibility of getting a bearish reaction from there. But keep in mind that this does not mean that every supply or demand zone should be traded blindly.  To identify a high probability supply or demand zone, we need to check multiple factors such as the strength of the zone, its freshness, price arrival and reaction signals.
And now we will understand step by step how to identify high probability supply and demand zones and when to trade these zones and when to avoid them.  To identify supply and demand zones, we need to look at some important factors.  First of all we have to see where the price has made a strong bullish move and where it has made a strong bearish move.
  The  candle that forms a swing low before the bullish move from where the price makes a strong bullish move is  called the demand zone.  Similarly, the  candle that is making a swing high before the bearish move from where the price has made a strong bearish move is  called the supply zone.
  But just looking at this does  n't give us a high probability supply or demand zone.  For this we also have to check some other important criteria.  First of all let us  understand the demand zone.  The price should move in the bullish direction with a strong displacement move from the candle which we are considering as the demand zone.
 We should also clearly see the imbalance during this bullish displacement. Also, a Fair Value Gap (FVG) should be formed along with the candle forming the demand zone. Similarly, in the supply zone, the price should move in the bearish direction with a strong displacement move from the candle which we are considering as the supply zone.
 During this bearish displacement, we should clearly see the imbalance.  Also, a Fair Value Gap (FVG) should be formed along with the candle forming the supply zone.  Additionally, when the price makes a strong bullish move after forming the demand zone, ideally the price should also break out of an important level or previous swing high.
This gives us confirmation that the bears' strength in the market was strong during that move. Similarly, when the price makes a strong bearish move after forming a supply zone, the price should also break down an important level or previous swing low. This gives us confirmation that the strength of sellers in the market was strong during that move.
So in simple language, when multiple criteria like strong displacement, imbalance, FVG and breakout or breakdown of important levels are aligning in a supply or demand zone , then we can consider that supply or demand zone as a high probability zone. But remember one thing here, high probability zone does not mean guaranteed reversal.
  The freshness of the zone, the arrival and reaction of the price are also important to check before taking a trade.  Let us understand with such an example.  For example, look at this chart.  Here the price has made a strong bearish move and a supply zone has formed before this move.  Now watch as the price retraces to this supply zone and taps the zone just slightly, giving a bearish pin bar, bearish engulfing or some other bearish reversal signal.
  Now the question is, should we enter a selling trade from here just by looking at this reversal signal  ?  No.  Because when a supply or demand zone is formed on the chart, we should not trade that zone until the price has tapped at least 50% of the area of ​​the zone.  If the price gives a bearish reversal signal before tapping the 50% area of ​​the supply zone , then we can consider such a signal as a weak or early reversal signal.
  Whose probability of failure is high.  In such situations, many times the price first gives a bearish reversal signal and then moves up a bit.   It taps the 50% area of ​​the supply zone and then continues its bearish move. Therefore, the supply zone should only be traded when the price  gives a strong bearish reversal signal after tapping at least 50% of the area of ​​the zone.
  When the price taps the 50% area and then gives bearish reversal confirmation then we can consider a sell trade from here.  Because in such a setup, the probability of bearish move continuation is high.  Now let us see how to identify the strongest setups in the supply and demand zones.  Look for a supply zone forming on the chart.
  From where the price comes down with a strong move. So this supply zone becomes even stronger when the price tries to reach this supply zone again. But without tapping it, it would have reversed and come down.  So now this supply zone becomes even stronger. Because now there is very high liquidity available above this swing high, just below this supply zone.
Now, when the price makes a retracement again, there is a high chance that the market will first hunt for liquidity above this swing high and then tap this supply zone and reverse down.  So, in such a situation, we can also enter a short trade from here after seeing any bearish reversal signal at this supply zone.
 Because in such a situation, the probability of the price moving bearishly from this supply zone is very high.  So hopefully, you now have a better understanding of how to identify a high probability supply and demand zone and when to trade it and when not to trade it. (60) 🔴Power of Higher Timeframe 

Transcripts:
Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  There are a lot of beginner traders.  When they're analyzing the charts [music], they see that when the market is moving in an uptrend, forming a bullish structure like this, they mark a bullish order block and wait for the price to reach this order block, so they'll buy from here.
  Now see, when the price comes to this order block, they immediately enter the buying trade from here. [Music] Let's place our stop loss below this order block and target the nearest swing high. When retailers buy here, the price often does not move in the direction of their trade but instead goes down, failing the order block.
  Now here those people get confused and start thinking that the market was bullish here. I also marked order blocks in the direction of the trend. Despite this,  why did the price fail the order block and hit my stop loss?  If this happens to you too and your order block fails even though it is in the direction of the trend, then it means that you are missing a very important thing and that is higher time frame confirmation.
  If you enter only on lower time frames and do not look at higher time frame charts then there is a very high chance that your [music] setup will fail.  Whether you use on-order blocks, fair value gaps, or anything else. That's why [music] we must look at higher time frame charts before taking entry on any setup. Because only higher time frames tell what the market can do next.
  So in today's video we are going to understand   why in most of the cases our stop loss gets hit when we take entry using only the lower time frame. You have to watch the video carefully and do not skip at all.  Only then will you understand everything well.  So let's start the video [music].  Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.
 Let us first start with the advanced market structure.  Only then will you understand everything clearly.  As told to you earlier, when the market is bullish, the price starts moving above a point. After going up a little, the buyers who had already made purchases start booking their profits.
  Because of this, we see a small pullback here. After the pullback, the bears become active again and buy again, pushing the price up.  Similarly, the price moves in an uptrend forming a bullish structure.  The  swing highs formed during this period are called higher highs. Because every new high is formed above its previous high and the swing lows that are formed are called higher lows because every new low is formed above its previous low.
Similarly, when the market is bearish, the price starts moving down from a point. After falling a little, the sellers who were already selling start booking their profits. Because of this, we see a small pullback here.  After the pullback, sellers become active again and push the price down by selling again.
Similarly, the price moves in a downtrend forming a bearish structure.   The swing lows formed during this period are called lower lows.  Because every new low is formed below its previous low and the swing highs formed are called lower highs. Because every new high is made below its previous high.
  Now let's understand combining this with a higher time frame. When the market is bullish on higher time frames, the price moves up making higher highs and higher lows. When we look at the same move on a lower time frame, the structure appears different to us.  The reason for this is that the impulsive bullish move on the higher time frames  appears as a strong up trend on the lower time frames with multiple higher highs and higher lows being formed.
Whereas a small pullback or correction on the higher time frame  becomes a complete bearish move on the lower time frame. In which the formation of lower highs and lower lows is clearly visible.  Similarly, when the market is bearish on higher time frames, the price moves downwards making lower lows and lower highs.
 When we look at the same move on a lower time frame, the structure appears different to us.  The reason for this is that the impulsive bearish move on the higher time frames  appears as a strong down trend on the lower time frames.  In which multiple lower lows and lower highs are formed.   At the same time, a small pullback or correction on the higher time frame  becomes a complete bullish move on the lower time frame.
In which the formation of higher highs and higher lows is clearly visible.  All this happens due to the fractal nature of the market. In which the structure of each time frame is  reflected differently on the lower time frame.  So you should always remember one thing that whenever you see the market trending on the lower time frame, whether it is an up trend or a down trend, you should not make the mistake of thinking that if the market is bullish on the lower time frame, then it will be bullish on the higher time frame as well.  What
happens in most cases is that the trend that appears clear to us on the lower time frame is actually just a small pullback on the higher time frame. In such a situation, if you trade only by looking at the lower time frame, then you assume that the market is bullish. As soon as the price reaches your marked point of interest zone, you immediately make a buy.  You place your stop loss below that zone and target the previous swing high.  But what happens?  The price moves slightly in the direction of your entry but is unable to sustain.  Then that order fails the block and your stop loss is hit.
As soon as the stop loss is hit, the market goes down further and you start blaming the market that the market always hunts your stop loss. Whereas there is no fault of the market here. Liquidity in trading means the availability of orders or stop losses in the market.  Liquidity exists where more traders have placed orders or placed stop losses.
  The market does not move randomly but moves towards those places where it finds liquidity.  Often liquidity is in areas where people commonly trade.  Such as above the previous high , below the previous low, above equal highs , below equal lows, above the resistance level , below the support level and above the high and below the low of each candle.
  Because many retail traders place their stop losses at these places.  For example, suppose there  is a support level here and when the price comes back to this support level, many retailers buy here and they keep their stop loss below the support level.  When retailers place their stop loss below the support. So this means that more liquidity is being created below the support level.
Now if smart money has to buy then smart money or big players  need more orders to execute their big trades. So they drive the price to these liquidity areas. When the price reaches there the first stop losses are hit which is called stop hunt or liquidity gap. Now, seeing the breakdown of support here, millions of retailers start selling.
Due to which the smart money gets enough sellers to buy its heavy orders and the smart money places its heavy buying orders here and its orders get filled easily. After this the market gets a strong move in the opposite direction.   For this reason, many times it seems that the market has trapped us and gone in the opposite direction.
In simple words, the price does not follow the direction.  Price follows liquidity.  A trader who understands liquidity also learns to trade with smart money. Let us now understand the structure of the candle so that you can also understand the liquidities of the candles.  As I have already told you that all the swing highs in the market have liquidity above them and all the lows have liquidity available below them.
But when we look at a single candle, it usually appears that there is neither any liquidity level nor any liquidity available.  This is where retailers make a mistake because they fail to understand the liquidity of candles.  Whereas within this single candle, both the liquidity level and liquidity are available.

When this candle starts forming, the price first opens at a point.  After that the sellers try to drive the price down. But the price does not go down much.
Here the buyers become active and push the price upwards by buying. When the price goes up, at one point again the sellers try to bring the price down. Whenever we see such a candle forming on a larger time frame , its structure breaks down on a smaller time frame and appears like this, where we can clearly see the liquidity levels.
Looking at this structure, we understand that the stop loss orders of the buyers are available below the low of this candle. Because when buyers have taken the price up by buying from here, they keep their stop loss below this low. And similarly, the seller's stop loss is available above the high of this candle.
Because when sellers sell here, they place their stop loss above this high. Similarly, when a bearish candle forms on the chart,  liquidity cannot be understood by looking at just one candle. After this retracement, bears again become active in the market and create buying pressure again.  Due to which the price moves upwards again. By repeating this process, the market forms a proper uptrend structure.
  In this bullish structure, every new top is formed above the previous top which we call higher high. And every new bottom is formed above the previous bottom which we know as higher low.  Now if we look at this concept in a bearish market, the scenario is exactly the opposite. When the market is weak, the price starts moving downwards.
After a slight drop, sellers who are already in sell positions book their profits. Due to which there is a small pullback in the price.  After the pullback is complete, sellers become active again and  push the price further down with selling pressure.  In this way the market gradually develops a down trend structure.
In this bearish structure, every new bottom is formed below the previous bottom which is called lower low and every new top is formed below the previous top which we call lower high.  Now that the basic market structure is clear, let's understand how we can combine this structure with higher time frames to make trades more accurate.
When the market is bullish on higher time frames, the price gradually moves upwards and makes higher highs and higher lows in the process.  But when we look at the same price movement on the lower time frame, the structure appears slightly different.  The reason for this is that the strong bullish move on the higher time frame  develops into a proper uptrend on the lower time frame.
  In which many higher highs and higher lows are clearly seen being formed.  And which is just a small pullback or correction on higher time frames.  The same thing looks like a complete bearish move on the lower time frames.  In which a structure of lower highs and lower lows is formed. In which the price structure of each time frame is  reflected in a different form on the time frame below it. If you pay attention, you will notice that most of the candles formed on higher time frames, when broken on lower time frames, almost always show a retracement at the end.
Now let us understand this concept  in more detail by aligning it with break and retrace.  For example, suppose the price is moving bearishly on the one-hour chart and a lower high has been formed here, which is acting as a resistance level.  When the price falls below this resistance level and we observe the same movement on the lower time frame, we see a structure like this.
  Now notice that when the price pulls back on the higher time frame and comes back near the resistance level, its structure looks quite different on the lower time frame. When a bullish candle forms on higher time frames, we see that the price first broke the resistance level but could not sustain above it and the candle closed below the resistance.
  When we analyze the same candle on the lower time frame , here too the price is seen breaking the resistance.  But because the candle on the higher time frame closed below resistance, this movement on the lower time frame only represents a retracement.  This is where retail traders often make mistakes. Traders who only look at lower time frame charts.
  They believe that the price has broken out of the resistance and will move higher after the retracement. With this thought in mind, they enter into the buying trade here. But see, instead of going up from here, the price moves down and all those traders who take entry only on the basis of lower time frame get trapped.  The main reason for this trap is that these traders focus only on the lower time frames and do not try to understand the market structure and behavior of the higher time frames.
   The  second reason is that these traders are planning to trade against the trend of higher time frames. Because the market is already bearish on the higher time frames and the bullish structure seen on the lower time frames is actually just a temporary pullback from the higher time frames. If you knew this technique of time frame integration, you would   not trade the lower time frame break and retest setup against the higher time frame trend, but would wait for the break and retest to fail and the price to pull back below the resistance.
After that, when you get a reversal signal on the lower time frame, you enter a short trade from here and by trading in the direction of the trend of the higher time frame, you can  capture good movement and better profit. [Music] Let's first understand the market structure in detail so that there is no confusion in understanding liquidity and order blocks later. Whenever the market moves , it always moves by making swing highs and swing lows.
  These swings are the turning points of the market. From where the price either reverses or continues the trend.  If the market is bullish, the price follows a clear pattern.  The market first makes a high. Then it retraces slightly and forms a low and then creates a new high above the previous high. In such moves , all the highs made in the market are above their previous highs and the lows are also above their previous lows.
  This indicates that buyers are strong and the market is trending in the upward direction.  Its opposite occurs in a bearish trend.  When the market is bearish, the price moves in the downward direction making swing highs and swing lows. During this period, the new high is always below the previous high and the low is also below the previous low.
  This clearly indicates that sellers are strong and the market is in a downtrend.  These swings are the simplest way to identify the market trend.  If both the highs and lows are formed above the previous level then the trend is bullish and if both are formed below the previous level then the trend is bearish.
  This observation helps in choosing the direction of trade in future.  Whether the market is in a bullish trend or a bearish trend. During this time, whatever swing highs and swing lows the market makes, whether they are small or big, there is a lot of liquidity above those swing highs and below those swing lows.
Because sellers have stop losses above the swing highs and buyers have stop losses below the swing lows. Understanding market structure is important because order blocks are always formed within the trend.  The order block is the area where a short-term reversal occurs and then continues in the direction of the market trend.
  But if you do not know whether the market is bullish or bearish, the order block will be marked wrong and the trade will move in the opposite direction.  That's why the first step is to identify the market structure. In which direction is the market making swing highs and swing lows?  When this clarity is present,  identifying order blocks and liquidity becomes crystal clear.
Now let us understand what order blocks are and how retail traders mark them incorrectly.  Think of an order block as a situation where big players in the market, such as banks, funds, or large investors, invest a lot of money in one direction.  When they do this, a sharp move up or down from that point is suddenly visible on the chart.
That place is what we call the order block. In simple words, this is the area from where the market started a big and strong move.  The price comes back to this area and reverses because that is where the big players are protecting their positions or placing new orders.  Therefore the market reacts and the direction may change.
Retail traders look at the chart and wherever the price has made a strong move, they mark the order block there and think that smart money is active here and when the price comes here, the smart money will reverse the price again.  Now see, when the price reaches this order block, these retailers start buying from here and place their stop loss below this order block.
After that, see, the price does not reverse from here and goes up but comes down and these retailers get trapped.  The price does not go up from here and comes down because smart money was not active on this order block.  Not all the big moves seen in the market are due to orders placed by smart money. Sometimes big moves also occur due to some news or big events.
That is why not every order block should be traded. Now this question might be coming in your mind that how do we know which order block is made up of smart money orders and on which order block smart money is active.  Which order block is composed of smart money orders and on which order block is smart money active.
  This is an important point to remember when identifying this.  Smart money does not place its orders at any random place, rather smart money places its orders where 90% of the people are getting trapped.  That means if smart money has to place its buying orders in the market, then they first take the price below some low.

Because when smart money takes the price above a high, retail traders start buying after seeing the breakout there. Smart money places heavy selling orders here and the price explodes from here and comes down and retail buyers get trapped. The level from where the price reversed should be unmitigated.

When the market makes a rapid impulsive move, the last opposite candle formed before that impulsive move is called an order block.
  Let's assume the market is moving in a bullish trend. During this time, whenever the price suddenly makes an impulsive upward move, we try to understand the institutional orders hidden behind that move.  The  last red candle formed just before this impulsive move is actually the place where big players like banks or institutions have done massive buying.
  So we draw a box from the high to the low of this entire red candle and this area is called the bullish order block.  But it is not necessary that the last red candle formed before every bullish impulsive move should be  marked as a bullish order block.  Many times such a scenario is seen in which the order block is marked  only on the first bullish candle of the impulsive move, leaving out the last red candle before the impulsive move.
  The  reason for this is that the low of this bullish candle often goes below the low of the last red candle.  That is, this candle hunts the liquidity of the low of the last red candle. Therefore, in this case, we do not consider the last red candle as an order block, but consider the bullish candle formed just before the impulsive move as an order block.

And if ever such a scenario arises in which just before the impulsive move, such a small body candle is formed and after that a strong bullish candle is formed which completely engulfs this small body candle and after that the impulsive move comes.
So in this case we take this small body candle and draw an order block by marking a zone like this till the low of this strong bullish candle.   When the imbalance aligns with the order block, it means that the price has moved sharply from here. Due to which proper buy orders could not be filled here.  This means that some orders are unfilled. Therefore, the price comes back to this area to fill the remaining orders and balance the imbalance.
  The second condition is that the price should break out of structure or change out of character after the order block is created. [Music] Let's start with the basics of market structure:  what exactly is market structure?  Market structure means looking at price highs and lows to understand whether the market is in an uptrend, downtrend, or moving sideways.
When the market is making every low below its previous low and every high below its previous high, it is called a bearish market structure.  Which means that the market is moving in a downtrend. When the price is moving in a downtrend, the price first moves down like this and then starts retracing from a particular point.
  When the price retraces to a point, this point becomes a low point.  Now after the retracement, when the price starts moving down again and breaks the previous low, it is called break of structure and this high becomes the lower high which means that the bearish trend has started.  Now the price goes down a bit and after reaching a certain point, it again starts giving upward retracement.
When the price retraces from here, this point becomes our lower low point because this low is formed below its previous low.  Now when the retracement is over the price goes down again and breaks its previous low and breaks off the structure and this high point becomes our lower high. Similarly, the price moves in a downtrend forming lower lows and lower highs.
 Here you have to remember that we will not consider this high as a lower high until the price breaks the previous low. If the price is unable to break the previous lower low, we will consider it only as a high. But if the price breaks the previous lower low then this high point becomes our lower high.
  And one more thing, the lower high point of the recent is also called major high.  Because if the price reverses from a lower level and comes up and breaks this lower high point, then it is called change of character.  Which means that now the sellers have become weak and the buyers have become strong and the price can now move in an up trend.
  Now here two questions might be coming in your mind.  The first is that in a downtrend market, if the price does not close below the previous low but sweeps this low with a pin bar candle and moves up, will we consider it a break of structure or not?  The answer is no.  If the price is moving in a downtrend and after the pullback, the price goes down but starts coming up by sweeping the liquidity below its previous low  and does not close below the previous low, then it will not be called a break of structure.
Because for a break-off structure, it is necessary that the candle closes below the previous low. If the price does not close below the previous low but sweeps it, then this is our first signal that sellers are getting weak and the market trend may change.  Now the second question that might be coming to your mind is that if in a down trend the market sweeps the previous low and comes up and breaks out this high, will we consider it a change of character?  The answer is yes, if the price does not close below this low
but sweeps it and comes up and breaks this high, then we will consider it as a change of character because here also the price, by not closing below the previous low, gives confirmation that the sellers have become weak and the buyers are becoming stronger.  If after this the price breaks its recent high and closes above it, then we will consider it as a change of character.
Which means that now the trend has changed. Now you have to mark any high probability fair value gap or order block. If the price gives a retracement after the change of character and shows a reversal signal on this order block then we can enter a long trade from here. Because in such conditions the chances of the price going up are very high.
Similarly, when the market is making every high above its previous high and every low above its previous low, then it is called bullish market structure which means that the market is moving in an up trend.  When the price is moving in an uptrend, the price first moves up like this and then starts retracing from a particular point.
  When the price retraces to a point, this point becomes a high point.  Now after the retracement, when the price starts moving up again and breaks the previous high, it is called break of structure.  And this low becomes a higher low which means that a bullish trend has started.  Now the price moves up a bit and after reaching a certain point, it again starts giving a retracement.
When the price retraces from here, this point becomes our higher high point because this high is formed above its previous high.  Now when the retracement is over the price again goes up and breaks its previous high and breaks off the structure and this low point becomes our higher low. Similarly, the price moves in an uptrend forming higher highs and higher lows.
 In this you always have to remember that we will not consider this low as a higher low until the price breaks the previous high. If the price has not broken the previous higher high then we will consider it only a low. But if the price breaks the previous higher high then this low point becomes our higher low point.
  And one more thing you have to remember is that the higher low point of the recent is also called major low. Because if the price reverses from a higher level and comes down and breaks this higher low point, then we call it change of character.  Which means that now buyers are weak and sellers have become strong and the price can move in an up-down trend.
  Now two questions might be coming to your mind.  The first is that in an uptrend market, if the price does not close above the previous high in this manner but instead sweeps this high with a pin bar candle and comes down, will we consider it a breakout structure or not?  The answer is no.
  If the price is moving in an uptrend and after the pullback, the price moves up but starts coming down after sweeping the liquidity above its previous high  and does not close above the previous high, then it will not be called a break of structure. Because for a break off structure it is necessary that the closing of the candle should be above the previous high.
If the price does not close above the previous high but sweeps it, then this is our first signal that now the bears are week and the market trend may change.  The second question that might be coming to your mind is that if in an uptrend the market sweeps the previous high and comes down and breaks this low, will we consider it a change of character?  The answer is yes, if the price does not close above this high but sweeps it down and breaks down this low, then we will consider it as a change of character because
here also the price, by not closing above the previous high, gives a confirmation that the buyers have become weak and the sellers are becoming stronger. If the price then breaks down to its recent low and closes below it, we will consider it a change of character. Which means that now the trend has changed.
  Now you have to mark any high probability fair value gap or order block.  If the price  makes a retracement after the change of character and shows a reversal signal on this order block, we can enter a short trade from here.  Because in such conditions the chances of the price going down are very high.  The same scenario happened in the chart that I showed at the beginning of the video in which you missed the entry.
  Let us now understand the logic of that chart, why your entry point was missed and how you could have traded it.  This is the chart I showed you at the beginning of the video where you missed your entry. In this you can see that the market had  moved in an up trend, forming a bullish structure.  In this you had marked the major higher low point here.
Because you have been told that a  new major low is not formed until the price breaks out of the structure.  That's why you had marked Change of Character Level here. But if you had knowledge of sweep technique then you would not have marked this low but would have marked this major low. Because the price has tried to move up from here but has not been able to close above the previous high.
This means that sellers are getting stronger and buyers are getting weaker. Now see, when the price has come down after sweeping the previous high, then this low has broken down and changed its character. Now we mark a breaker block and an order block here and wait for the price to retracement and  enter the short trade from whichever level gives the reversal signal.
Now that the price has given a retracement, we see a bearish engulfing candlestick forming on the breaker block, which signals a bearish reversal.  So now we can enter a short trade from here. After that, see that the price has started moving in a down trend from here.  This way you could trade it.
  If you thought of trading the change of character by marking this low, you would not have been able to trade it. Because first of all, this was not a change of character level.  And the second thing is that even if we considered it a change of character level, we would still not have been able to enter. Because when the price has broken down, it has  gone down without any retracement.
  So you always have to remember that if the price is unable to close above its previous higher high and sweeps it and breaks down to the recently formed low then we can consider it as a change of character. Because when liquidity is swept above the previous higher high, it means that bears are no longer able to push the price higher. And immediately after that, if the price breaks down below the recent low, then this gives us further confirmation that now the sellers have become stronger and now the price can move downwards.
You will not see this on the chart every day, but whenever you see it, you have to understand its logic and trade it in this manner. (60) 🔴 Valid & Invalid pullbacks


Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  Right now you are seeing a chart in front of you in which the price is  moving in an uptrend forming a bullish structure.  See, the price goes up after giving a pullback here and then when the price gets rejected from some important level and comes down, it breaks down this low.
Now you look here and think that the price has changed its character i.e. the trend has changed now and now we have to focus on short trades. Now here you mark the order block and wait that as soon as the price reaches that order block, you will enter the short trade.  Now see, after the change of character, the price gives a pullback and comes on the order block marked by you, then from here you enter the short trade.
But let's see what happens next.  The price does not reverse from the order block but moves opposite your entry and you get trapped.  Now you start wondering what mistake you made that caused your stop loss to be hit.  The  biggest reason why your stop loss gets hit here is that you were not able to identify a valid pullback.
  Here, the pullback whose breakdown of low value you considered as change of character, that pullback was an invalid pullback.  This is why your stop loss was hit.  So in today's episode we are going to understand this topic in detail that what is pullback?  How are valid pullbacks identified? What is the difference between a valid and invalid pullback?  And  why is it important for every trader to identify valid pullbacks?  So, as always, in today's episode too, you are going to learn a lot of new and advanced things.  So
you have to watch the video carefully and till the end.  Only then will you understand everything well. So let's start the video.  Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.  [Music] [Music] First, let's understand what pullbacks are. In simple words, a pullback is a move that is opposite to the overall trend.
  But only for a short period of time like a small pause, after which the price again starts moving in its main trend. If the market is moving bullish, then during this time the price temporarily  moves in the opposite direction of its major trend.  And after that, if it again starts moving in its main trend, then this temporary opposite move is called pullback.
  Similarly, when the market is moving bearishly, if the price temporarily moves in the opposite direction of its major trend and then starts moving again in its main trend, then this temporary opposite move is called a pullback.  But not all the pullbacks we see on the chart are  useful to us because many pullbacks on the chart are valid and many are invalid.
  If we use a valid pullback, it gives us a better entry opportunity and prevents us from taking the trade at the wrong place. But if we use invalid pullback then there is a high  chance of us incurring loss.  Therefore, it is important to know how to identify valid and invalid pullbacks. So let us now understand how to identify valid and invalid pullbacks.
In this video we will understand eight types of pullbacks.  If you understand all these well then you will never face  any problem in pullback identification and structure mapping.  So now you have to understand the video carefully.  A valid pullback in an uptrend market occurs when the price is moving bullish and the low of the previous candle whose high is higher during the pullback  must be broken.
  Whether from the wick or from the candle close.  The candle that breaks the low can be either bullish or bearish. If the low of the high-forming candle is not broken, the pullback will be considered invalid. Similarly, in a downtrend market, a valid pullback is formed when the price is moving bearishly. And during the pullback, the  high of the previous candle whose low is the lowest must be broken.
Whether from the wick or from the candle close.  The candle that breaks the high can be either bullish or bearish. If the high of the low forming candle is not broken then the pullback will be considered invalid.  Now let us understand the different types of pullbacks so that you understand everything about pullbacks.
  Our first type of pullbak looks something like this. When the market is moving bullish, the price makes an impulsive move. After that, when the price starts pulling back, the biggest high during this period is of this last green candle only.  Therefore, for this pullback to be valid, the low of the candle making this high must be broken.
Now when the next candle is formed and this candle breaks the low of that candle then this pullback becomes valid. After that the price moves up again and breaks out of the structure. Whenever such pullbacks are seen on the chart in which the low of the candle making the high is broken, then it is considered a valid pullback.
Similarly, when the market is moving bearishly, the price makes an impulsive move. After that, when the price starts pulling back, the biggest low during this period is of this last red candle.  Therefore, for this pullback to be valid, the high of the candle forming this low must be broken.  Now when the next candle is formed and this candle breaks the high of that candle , then this pullback becomes valid.
After that the price goes down again and breaks out of the structure. Whenever such pullbacks are seen on the chart, in which the high of the candle forming the low is broken , it is considered a valid pullback. Now let us understand the next type of pullback. Our second type of pullback looks something like this.
In this we see that when the market is moving bullish, the price starts giving a pullback after making an impulsive move. Here the high of the last green candle and the first red candle are equal. So, during a pullback, whenever we see two or more candles having equal highs, then for the pullback to be valid, the  last candle among the candles forming equal highs must break the low.
  During this time, when the next candle is formed and breaks the low of that candle either by the candle body close or by the wick, then this pullback becomes valid. After this the price moves up again and breaks out of the structure. So whenever you see such a pullback on the chart, you have to understand that it is a valid pullback.
  Similarly, when the market is moving bearishly, the price starts pulling back after making an impulsive move. Here the low of the last red candle and the first green candle are equal. So during a pullback, whenever we see the lows of two or more candles being equal. So, for the pullback to be valid, the last candle among the candles making equal losses  must break its high.
  During this time, when the next candle is formed and breaks the high of that candle either by the candle body close or by the wick, then this pullback becomes valid. After this the price goes down again and breaks out of the structure. So whenever you see such a pullback on the chart, you have to understand that it is a valid pullback.
  Now let us understand the next type of pullback.  Our third type of pullback looks something like this. In this we see that when the market is moving bullish, the price makes an impulsive move.  After that a red candle is formed whose high is higher than this last green candle. In this condition, for the pullback to be valid, the low of that red candle must be broken.
Because I told you earlier that a pullback is considered valid only when the low of the candle making the highest high is broken. Here too, the high of this red candle remains the biggest.  Therefore, for a pullback to be valid, it must have a low break.  Now see, a candle is formed which first breaks the low of this red candle and then goes up.
  So we will call this pullback a valid pullback because the low of this red candle has been broken.  Similarly, when the market is moving bearishly, the price makes an impulsive move.  After that a green candle is formed whose low is even lower than this last red candle. In this condition, the high of that green candle must be broken for the pullback to be valid.
Because I told you earlier that a pullback is considered valid only when the high of the candle that formed the lowest low is broken. Here also the low of this green candle remains the smallest.  Therefore, for a pullback to be valid, it must break high. Now see, a candle is formed which first breaks the high of this green candle and then goes down.
  So we will call this pullback a valid pullback because the high of this green candle has been broken.  Now let us understand the next type of pullback. Our fourth type of pullback looks something like this. In this we see that when the market is in a bullish trend and during this time the price makes an impulsive move, after that when it starts giving a pullback, during this time the high of the last green candle is the highest.
This green candle must break the low for the pullback to be valid. Now see a candle is formed which breaks the low of the green candle with its wick.  As soon as this candle breaks the low of the green candle. This pullback becomes a valid pullback. After this the price moves up and breaks out of the structure.
  Whenever we see such pullbacks on the charts, they are considered valid pullbacks. Similarly, when the market is in a bearish trend, the price makes an impulsive move. After that, when it starts giving a pullback, the low of the last red candle is the lowest during this time.  This red candle must break high for the pullback to be valid.
Now see a candle forming which breaks the high of the red candle with its wick. As soon as this candle breaks the high of the red candle , this pullback becomes a valid pullback. After this the price goes down and breaks out of the structure.  Whenever we see such pullbacks on the charts, they are considered valid pullbacks.
Now let us understand the next type of pullback. Our fifth type of pullback looks something like this. In this we see that when the market is moving uptrend forming a bullish structure, then when the price starts giving a pullback, then when the first red candle is formed after the last green candle, then this red candle is completely formed within the high and low of the last green candle.
We also call it inside bar candle.  Now when the third candle starts forming, the price breaks the low of the first inside bar candle. After that he goes upstairs.  So we will not call this a valid pullback because this is a green candle making a high and the low of this green candle has not been broken.
  Therefore, we will consider this pullback as an invalid pullback.   That means this entire move is an impulsive move. Similarly, when the market is moving in a downtrend forming a bearish structure, when the price starts giving a pullback, then after the last red candle, when the first green candle is formed, then this green candle is completely formed within the high and low of the last red candle.
We also call it inside bar candle. Now when the third candle starts forming, the price first breaks the high of the inside bar candle and then moves down.  So we will not call this a valid pullback because this is a red candle making a low and the high of this red candle has not been broken.  Therefore, we will consider this pullback as an invalid pullback.
   That means this entire move is an impulsive move. Now let us understand the next type of pullback. Our sixth type of pullback looks something like this.  When the market is moving in an uptrend forming a bullish structure and the price starts pulling back after an impulsive move,  a red candle is formed after the last green candle.
After this, a red candle is formed again which is a pin bar candle.  After the formation of a pin bar candle, the price moves up again. In this case, we will consider it an invalid pullback because the candle making the high is this green candle and its low has not been broken. Therefore this pullback is not valid and will be considered as an invalid pullback.
  Similarly, when the market is moving in a downtrend forming a bearish structure and the price starts pulling back after an impulsive move, a green candle is formed after the last red candle. After this, a green candle is formed again which is a pin bar candle. After the formation of a pin bar candle, the price moves down again.
  In this case also we will call it an invalid pullback because the candle forming the low is a red candle and its high has not been broken.  Therefore this pullback is not valid and will be considered as an invalid pullback. Now let us understand the next type of pullback.  In this, we see that when the market is moving in an uptrend forming a bullish structure, then when the price starts giving a pullback after an impulsive move, then that pullback is something like this in which we see multiple red candles being formed like this.
When these red candles are formed, it breaks the low of the last red candle and starts moving bullish.  So we call this pullback also an invalid pullback because this green candle has made a high and the low of this green candle has not been broken.  Therefore, we 
will consider this entire move as a single move i.e. an impulsive move.  Similarly, when the market is moving in a downtrend forming a bearish structure, then when the price starts giving a pullback after an impulsive move, then that pullback is something like this in which we see multiple green candles being formed like this.  When these green candles are formed, the price starts moving bearish by breaking the high of the last green candle.
So we call this pullback also an invalid pullback because this red candle has made a low and the high of this red candle has not been broken.  Therefore, we will consider this entire move as a single move i.e. an impulsive move.  So you always have to remember that for a valid pullback in a bullish market, the candle that formed the high must break the low.
Whether from Vick or from a body close.  Similarly, in a bearish market, for a pullback to be valid, it is necessary that the high of the candle that formed the low should be broken.  Whether from Vick or from a body close.  Only then will we consider it a valid pullback.
  The change of character that has occurred in the chart that I showed you at the beginning of the video is  not a valid change of character because the price has broken down an invalid pullback and changed the character. So your stop loss was hit here. I hope now you have understood how to identify valid and invalid pullbacks very easily.
