(60) 🔴Change in state of delivery Model📊‼


Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel.  Where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  Many times we see that when the market is moving in a downtrend like this and during this time when the price changes its character, then we start marking zones like order block and fair value gap and wait that when the price comes to any of these zones, then we will enter the trade on the buying side.
But what happens next? See, the price moves up without touching our levels and we miss the entry. Now we start wondering what was it that prevented the price from coming down.  The main reason for the price not coming down after the change of character is the change in the state of delivery.  The main reason for you missing the entry here is that you do not know the meaning of change in state of delivery.
If you had knowledge of this concept, you would have executed the trade before the change of character occurred. So in today's episode we are going to understand this concept in detail. What is change in state of delivery? How to recognize it?  What is the difference between market structure shift and change in state of delivery ?  What are the criteria that  make CISD a high probability concept?  And finally, we will learn  how to take high probability trades using this concept.
  So you have to watch this video carefully till the end so that you can understand everything well. Let's start the video.  Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.  [Music] [Music] So let's first understand what a change in state of delivery is. In simple words, change in state of delivery is a reversal pattern.
  If the price is moving in an uptrend and this pattern is formed, it means that the price may move up or down.   On the other hand, if the price is in a downtrend and this pattern is formed, it means that the price can now move in the upward direction.  For example, suppose here we  see the market moving in an uptrend, forming a bullish structure.
  Now the four candles seen here have  broken the recent market structure and created a break of structure.  These candles are showing recent buy side delivery.  That means these candles have pushed the price up.  So right now, the first bullish candle of this bullish move that we are seeing, the opening point of that candle will be our CISD level.
  If there was a red candle in the middle instead of the green candle, then we would consider the open point of this green candle as the CISD level.  Because for CISD in a bullish market we have to mark the open point of the first bullish candle of the last impulsive move. So now we mark the open point of this first bullish candle.
Now if the price gives a small retracement but does not reverse and closes below the open point of the first green candle of this last bullish move, then we call it a change in state of delivery.  This means that now the market momentum has shifted from buy side delivery to sell side and now the buyers have become weak and the sellers have become strong.
  This is a strong trend reversal signal that the market may now move downwards.  Now to trade this, you have to see that when the price changes into the state of delivery in a bullish market, the level which was earlier support now starts acting as resistance. Now you have to see that after CCD, when the price gives a retracement and signals a bearish reversal at any resistance fair value gap or on order block, then you can take a short trade from there.
  Because in such a setup, the probability of the price moving downwards from there is very high. Now let us understand this in a bearish context also.  Now suppose here we see the market moving in a downtrend, forming a bearish structure.  The five candles formed here have  broken the recent market structure and created a break-off structure.
These candles are showing recent sell side delivery.  That means these candles have pushed the price downwards.  Now that we see the first bearish candle of this bearish move , the opening point of that candle will be our CISD level.  If there was a green candle in the middle instead of the red candle, then we would have considered the open point of this red candle as the CISD level.
  Because for CISD in a bearish market we need to mark the open point of the first bearish candle of the last impulsive move. So now we mark the open point of this first bearish candle. Now if the price gives a small retracement but does not reverse and closes above the open point of the first red candle of this last bullish move, then  we call it a change in state of delivery.
This means that now the market momentum has shifted from sell side delivery to buy side and now the sellers have become weak and the buyers have become strong. This signals a strong trend reversal that the market may now move bullish.  Now to trade this, you have to see that in a bearish market, when the price changes into the state of delivery, the level which was earlier resistance now starts acting as support.
  Now you have to see that after CISD when the price gives a retracement and indicates a bullish reversal at any support level fair value gap or order block then you can enter the long trade from there. Because in such a setup, the probability of the price moving bullish from there is quite high.  Now let us understand  what is the difference between change in state of delivery and change of character.
  A change of character in a downtrend market is considered when the price breaks the recently formed lower high.  But we often see changes in the state of delivery suddenly. And many times we get its signal even before the change of character.   That is, when the market is moving in the downward direction, the change of character is confirmed only when the price breaks the previous lower high.
But the CISD signal comes when the price breaks the opening of the first red candle formed after the last green candle. That is why CISD is considered an early reversal indication.  But we cannot use the CISD pattern just by looking at it, rather it is necessary to fulfill some extra conditions which make this pattern more reliable.
  So let us now understand in detail the conditions that make CISD a powerful pattern.   The first condition that makes CISD more reliable is CISD plus higher time frame rejection model.  In this, we see if the price is rejecting from any reaction zone like fair value gap, order block, supply demand or support resistance on higher time frame like 1 hour.
And during this time, if we see a Change in State of Delivery pattern forming on a lower time frame like 5 minutes, then we can trade it confidently.  If CISD forms at such a level, it means that the market may indeed reverse. But if this pattern is formed at a random place where there is no level then its reliability becomes very low.
  Which we should avoid.  The second condition that makes CISD more reliable is power reversal.  In this we see that if the price reverses with strong movement and goes on creating imbalance and forms CISD, then we consider this CISD to be more reliable.  Because when the price reverses with strong momentum and leaves behind an imbalance, it means that a strong participant has become active in the market who is changing the direction of the price.
After such a reversal movement, if the candle clearly shifts delivery then that CISD becomes a high probability trade signal.  Conviction is higher in this type of CISD because the price has already shown an aggressive move in which many weak participants are trapped. After CISD, if the price retraces a little and reaches an imbalance order block or any other reaction zone and shows a reversal signal, then we can confidently plan the trade from here because in such a situation the chances of the price reversing from there are
very high. For example, you can see on this chart that the price first moved in an upward trend forming a bullish structure here and after that the price started consolidating in a small range.  So now if we talk about the CISD candle, then this one is a green candle because a red candle has formed here and after that the price has started going up from here and has started consolidating in a range here.
We are not considering the green candles formed inside this range as CISD candles because the entire range formed is completely inside this green candle. So we are marking the opening of the first green candle after this red candle as the CISD level. Now see, the price has taken a strong rejection from here and has come downwards and has broken down the open point of this first green candle and has become CISD.
  Now if you observe, you will find that when the price has come down from here, the price has come down leaving multiple imbalances.  This means that the price has come down with powerful moves. From which we come to know that now a big participant has entered the market. So now we're marking all the possible reaction zones here.
  Now see, when the price has come to this breaker block, here we see the formation of Bearish Harami candlestick pattern. So now we can enter a short trade from here. Our stop loss will be above the breaker block and our target will be at the nearest swing low point.  Now after that you can see that the price has moved strongly from here and has gone downwards.
  Now let us understand the next condition.  The third condition that makes CCD more reliable is liquidity sweep.  In this we see that suppose the price has  come in an up trend forming a bullish structure like this and here we see equal highs being formed. So this means that there is a liquidity pool available above these equal highs.
  This means that many sellers have placed stop losses above these equal highs. Now you have to see that if the price  sweeps the liquidity above these equal highs and then forms a CISD pattern, then we consider this CISD pattern to be more reliable because in such a setup the market first deliberately hunts the stop loss due to which the weak sellers exit their positions and some traders take long positions thinking it to be a breakout.
But when the price immediately reverses and forms a CISD pattern, it is a clear signal that the market has just collected liquidity and now the real direction of the price may be downwards. After such a CISD, if the price retraces slightly and reaches an order block or any other reaction zone and a reversal signal is received there, then we can confidently enter a short trade from here.
 For example, you can see on this chart that the price has first moved up, forming a bullish structure. Equal highs appear to be forming. That is why the price went up and a strong bullish candle was formed. But the behavior of the second candle that has formed is slightly different.   After that this candle closed bullish.  We often see such candlesticks when the market tries to reverse.  But there is no actual reversal. So if we understand the psychology of this candlestick, then when the first candle is formed on the chart, then at that time the buyers are very strong. Due to which a strong bullish candle is formed.
  But one thing you always have to remember is that sometimes instead of this first green candle, we can also see a red i.e. bearish candle. When the second candle begins to form , as soon as the price opens, sellers are the first to become active in the market. Sellers resort to heavy selling to control the price.  Due to which the price goes down and breaks the low of the first candle and the liquidity below the low of the first candle gets swept away.
  After that the buyers start becoming active again in the market and do heavy buying.  Due to which strong buying pressure is seen in the price.   The price moves back up and breaks the high of the first candle and closes, and the second candle also forms a strong bullish candle with a long lower wick, indicating that the bears have taken control of the market.
As you know, when the lower wick of a candle is long, it means that the pressure from bears is strong and the price can move bullish further. So here too the probability of the market moving bullish is high.  When the second candle breaks the low of the first candle, we call it manipulation.  Because here, along with buyers, many sellers also get trapped.
  To understand this, let us draw the structure of these two candles so that you can understand it better.  So right now as we can see here we see the first candle remaining bullish. When we draw the structure of this first candle in the form of a line, it looks something like this in which the open, low, high and close can all be clearly identified.
  Now see, when the next candle starts forming on the chart, the price first goes down and breaks the low of the first candle. As soon as the price breaks the low of the first candle, many buyers who had bought from here, their stop loss gets hit.  And many new sellers, seeing the breakdown, think that the market will now go down.
  That's why they sell and place their stop loss higher. When many retailers have sold here, then buyers become active and start taking the price up by strong buying. Because of this,  all the sellers who have sold at breakdown get trapped.  The price then breaks the high of the first candle and makes a strong bullish close.
  Whenever this condition arises in the market, it indicates that the bears are quite strong in the market at this time and it can move bullish in the future also. So now we have to enter the trade on the buy side.  Before understanding how we should trade it, let us understand it in a bearish context as well. After that we will learn how to trade it.
So right now we have two bearish candles forming.  The first candle means that sellers were active in the market at that time. That is why the price went down and a strong bearish candle was formed.  But the behavior of the second candle that has formed is slightly different.  This candle tells us that the price first went upside and swept the liquidity above the high of the first candle.

  After that we have to switch this chart to a lower time frame. When we switch the chart to the lower time frame, we see the structure of these two candles forming something like this.   If we come across any reaction zone like supply or resistance near the low of the second candle or any reaction zone near the close of the second candle and we see a signal of a reversal candle there, then we can enter a short trade from there. We need to place our stop loss slightly above that reaction zone and target either the nearest swing low or double our stop loss.
In this condition, the probability of the price going down is very high. (60) 🔴Dual Trap Trading setup

Transcripts:
Hello friends, welcome to PKR Trading channel where we explain all the complex topics of finance and stock market in a simple way.  Does this happen to you too when you are looking at charts ?  Whenever you see a breakout anywhere, you immediately enter the buy side trade. But when you enter a buy trade here, the price reverses from here and  moves in the opposite direction of your trade and your stop loss is hit.
  Now you see again that the price appears to be giving a breakdown again. Now you think that the price is going to go down from here. Now you enter the short trade here again. But after that the price again reverses from here and goes up and here you get trapped again. Why does this happen to you?  In today's episode, we are going to discuss a very powerful concept related to this,  which is called Dual Trap Theory.
  In today's episode, we are going to understand in detail what is Dual Trap Theory? Why is dual trap theory needed ?  What are the types of Dual Trap and how should we trade Dual Trap ?  So you have to watch the video carefully till the end and do not skip the video at all.  Only then will you understand everything well.
  So let's start the video.  Please like the video and if you are new to this channel then please subscribe to the channel.  [Music] [Music] Let's first understand what dual trap theory means.  The Dual Trap Theory describes a price action move in which the market first hunts the stop loss of buyers and then the stop loss of sellers before the actual movement begins.
That is, we can say that the market first traps traders from both sides and takes them out of the market and then gives a directional move. We call this price behavior the Dual Trap Theory.  For example, suppose the price is moving in an uptrend here, forming higher highs and higher lows. Now watch as the price breaks one of its resistances here.
Seeing this breakout, many retailers start buying here. When a lot of retailers buy here, the price suddenly reverses and all these retail buyers get trapped.  Now see, when the price comes down from here and breaks down its previous swing low, then here again many retail traders seeing the change of character think that now the trend has reversed and the price will go down from here.
  Seeing this, they start selling.  When enough retailers sell here, the price reverses again and starts moving in its original direction.  Similarly, suppose the price is moving in a downtrend here, forming lower highs and lower lows. Now watch as the price breaks down one of its supports here. Seeing this breakdown, many retailers start selling here.
When a lot of retailers make sales here, the price suddenly reverses and all these retailers get trapped.  Now see, when the price comes up from here and breaks out its previous swing high, then here again many retail traders seeing the change of character think that now the trend has reversed and the price will go up from here.
  Seeing this, they start buying.  When a large number of retailers buy here, the price reverses again and starts moving in its original direction i.e. down trend. And this price behavior is what we call the Dual Trap Theory.  Now let us understand why dual trap model is needed.  It often happens in trading that we see a breakout or breakdown and take a quick entry.
  But within a short time the price starts moving in the opposite direction and our stop loss gets hit. In such situations, most traders get frustrated and think that the market has cheated them.  Actually this is not the normal behavior of the price.  Rather it is a manipulation.  Where the market first traps traders on both sides and then moves in the original direction.
  The dual trap model helps us understand this manipulation. With its help we can know when the market is just trapping and when it is really going to give a strong move. This not only improves our entries but also prevents us from getting stuck at the wrong place.  So let us now understand what types of Dual Trap models there are and how we should trade them.  There are mainly two types of dual traps.
  The first type is dual trap candlestick patterns. Dual Trap candlestick patterns are multiple candle price action patterns in which price gobbles up liquidity in both directions and traps both buyers and sellers.  This pattern  involves some specific candlestick formations.  In which we see liquidity disappearing from both sides.
 Our first pattern of dual trap candlestick patterns is the bullish and bearish engulfing candlestick pattern.  The bullish engulfing pattern is a formation of two candles.  The first candle is a small bearish one and the second candle is a strong bullish one that completely engulfs the pure body of the first candle.
If we understand the psychology of this candlestick, then when the first red candle is formed then there is more selling pressure in the market.  This results in the formation of a small bearish candle.  But when the next session starts, sellers again try to drive the price down. During this time, the price goes to the low of the first candle and the liquidity there disappears.
After that the buyers become active from there and start aggressive buying. Due to this buying pressure, the price also breaks the high of the first red candle. After which the liquidity of the high of the red candle also disappears.  In the end, the price closes as a strong green candle that engulfs the entire red candle.
  In this way, liquidity is lost on both the upside and downside, and the buyers trap the sellers.  This is why I have categorized this candlestick formation as a dual trap pattern. How to trade it?  Let's understand this.  To trade the bullish engulfing pattern, you don't have to use only lower time frame charts.
  Rather, along with this, higher time frames also have to be analyzed.  This means that you first need to  identify the bullish engulfing pattern on a higher time frame and then plan your entry on a lower time frame. Let's say we are looking at the chart on a 30-minute time frame. Where we see a bullish engulfing pattern forming. Now if we analyze the structure within this pattern on the lower time frame, we can understand the price movement in detail.
 You should not enter directly when the next candle starts forming after bullish engulfing.  You need to wait for the price to reach a fair value gap order block or support level on the lower time frame.  If the price reaches these key zones and a reversal candlestick pattern forms there, you can take a buy side trade from there.
   A bullish engulfing pattern means that the market is currently under the control of bears. Therefore this pattern is considered a strong bullish signal.  And if this bullish engulfing pattern is formed at the support level of a reaction zone such as the demand zone , then the success probability of this trade increases even more.
  Similarly, the Bearish Engulfing pattern is a formation of two candles.  The first candle is a small bullish one and the second candle is a strong bearish one that completely engulfs the body of the first candle. If we understand the psychology of this candlestick, then when the first green candle is formed then there is more buying pressure in the market.
This results in the formation of a small bullish candle. But when the next session starts, the bears again try to push the price up. During this time, the price reaches the high of the first candle and the liquidity there disappears.  After that the sellers become active from there and start aggressive selling.
  Due to this selling pressure, the price also breaks the low of the first green candle.  After which the liquidity of the low of the green candle also disappears. In the end, the price closes as a strong red candle that engulfs the entire green candle.  In this way, liquidity is lost on both the upside and downside. This is why I have categorized this candlestick formation as a dual trap pattern.
How to trade it ?  Let's understand this. To trade the bearish engulfing pattern, you don't have to use only lower time frame charts. Rather, along with this, higher time frames also have to be analyzed.   That means you have to first identify the bearish engulfing pattern on the higher time frame and then plan the entry on the lower time frame.
Let's say we are looking at the chart on a 30-minute time frame.  Where we see a bearish engulfing pattern forming. Now if we analyze the structure within this pattern on the lower time frame, we can understand the price movement in detail.  You should not enter directly when the next candle starts forming after the bearish engulfing.
  You need to wait for the price to reach a fair value gap, order block or resistance level on the lower time frame.  If the price reaches these key zones and a reversal candlestick pattern forms there, you can take a selling side trade from there.  The bearish engulfing pattern means that the market is currently under the control of sellers.
Therefore this pattern is considered a strong bearish signal.  And if this bearish engulfing pattern  is formed at the resistance level of a reaction zone such as the supply zone, then the success probability of this trade increases even more. Now our second pattern of dual trap candlestick is the long wick rejection candle.
  In this we see that whenever we  see a red or green candle forming on the chart.  For example, suppose there is a red candle here.  Now when the next candle opens, the price goes down to break the low of the red candle. But in the end, heavy buying pressure comes in this candle. Due to which this candle comes back up and closes within the range of the previous red candle, which becomes a long lower week rejection candle.
  This tells us that the liquidity below the low of the red candle has been swept away and now it is the  turn of the liquidity sweep to the high of the previous red candle.  This means that now the price can go towards the high of this red candle to lose its liquidity. So in this situation we have to  plan a buying side trade on the lower time frame.
  How to trade a long wick rejection candle?  If you want to understand this in detail then you can watch this episode of Power of Candle Closer. Where we have taught how to trade it in great detail. Now let us understand the second type of dual trap. Our second type of dual trap model is the market structure dual trap.  In this we see that suppose here on the lower time frame the market is moving in an upper trend by forming higher highs and higher lows.
 Price has made a swing high here and a swing low here. Now watch as the price moves up and breaks its swing high. Due to which all the stop losses placed by the sellers at this swing high get hit and many new buyers start buying after seeing the breakout here.  When a lot of buyers buy here , the price reverses from here and comes down and all the buyers get trapped.
After that when the price comes down and breaks down its swing low also. So here also, all the stop losses of the bears which are placed below this swing low , all those stop losses get hit. And here many new sellers, after seeing the breakdown, start selling thinking it to be a change of character. Because they feel that the price is going to go down from here.
  When many sellers sell here , the price reverses from here and starts following its main trend and the  stop losses of all the sellers who have sold here are hit. How is Change of Character traded ?  You have been taught about this in detail in the previous episodes. That's why for now we will only learn how to trade the Dual Trap.
So to trade this dual trap, you have to see that whenever the market changes its  character after breaking off the structure on the lower time frame, you should not immediately enter the reversal trade there. You need to see if the price comes back above the breakout level within a few candles immediately after the change of character and then immediately check your chart on a higher time frame.
  For example, suppose we are currently looking at the chart of 5 minute lower time frame, then for higher time frame we have to use 30 minute time frame.  If on the 30-minute timeframe we see  a long lower wick rejection candle forming after the liquidity sweep below the low of a red candle, then you need to come back to the lower timeframe and wait for the price to cross above the Church level, from here you can enter a buy side trade.
  In this you have to  keep your stop loss below this swing low and your target should be at the nearest swing high point or double your stop loss. In this situation, the probability of the price going up from here is very high.  Now similarly, suppose here on the lower time frame the market  is moving in a down trend forming lower highs and lower lows.
Price makes a swing low here and a swing high here.  Now watch as the price moves down and breaks its swing low. Due to which all the stop losses placed by the buyers below this swing low get hit and many new sellers start selling after seeing the breakdown here. When many sellers sell here, the price reverses from here and comes up and all the sellers get trapped.
  After that when the price moves up and breaks out its swing high also.  So here also, all the stop losses of the sellers which are placed above this swing high, all those stop losses get hit.  And here many new buyers, seeing the breakout, start buying it thinking it to be a change of character. Because they feel that the price is going to go up from here.
  When a lot of buyers buy here, the price reverses from here and starts following its main trend and the stop loss of all the buyers who have bought here gets hit.  So to trade this dual trap, you have to see that whenever the market changes its character after breaking off the structure on the lower time frame,  you should not immediately enter the reversal trade there.
You need to see if the price comes back below the breakout level within a few candles immediately after the change of character and then immediately check your chart on a higher time frame. For example, suppose we are currently looking at the chart of 5 minute lower time frame, then for higher time frame we have to use 30 minute time frame.
  If on the 30-minute timeframe we see  a long upper week rejection candle forming after a liquidity sweep above the high of a green candle, then you need to come back to the lower timeframe and wait for the price to enter a sell side trade as soon as it crosses below the George level.  In this, you have to keep your stop loss above this swing high and your target should be at the nearest swing low point or double your stop loss.
In this situation, the probability of the price going down from here is very high.  So, this was our detailed episode on Dual Trap Theory. I hope you have understood everything well. There are mainly two types of dual traps.
  The first type is dual trap candlestick patterns. Dual Trap candlestick patterns are multiple candle price action patterns in which price gobbles up liquidity in both directions and traps both buyers and sellers.  This pattern  involves some specific candlestick formations.  In which we see liquidity disappearing from both sides.
 Our first pattern of dual trap candlestick patterns is the bullish and bearish engulfing candlestick pattern.  The bullish engulfing pattern is a formation of two candles.  The first candle is a small bearish one and the second candle is a strong bullish one that completely engulfs the pure body of the first candle.
If we understand the psychology of this candlestick, then when the first red candle is formed then there is more selling pressure in the market.  This results in the formation of a small bearish candle.  But when the next session starts, sellers again try to drive the price down. During this time, the price goes to the low of the first candle and the liquidity there disappears.
After that the buyers become active from there and start aggressive buying. Due to this buying pressure, the price also breaks the high of the first red candle. After which the liquidity of the high of the red candle also disappears.  In the end, the price closes as a strong green candle that engulfs the entire red candle.
  In this way, liquidity is lost on both the upside and downside, and the buyers trap the sellers.  This is why I have categorized this candlestick formation as a dual trap pattern. How to trade it?  Let's understand this.  To trade the bullish engulfing pattern, you don't have to use only lower time frame charts.
  Rather, along with this, higher time frames also have to be analyzed.  This means that you first need to  identify the bullish engulfing pattern on a higher time frame and then plan your entry on a lower time frame. Let's say we are looking at the chart on a 30-minute time frame. Where we see a bullish engulfing pattern forming. Now if we analyze the structure within this pattern on the lower time frame, we can understand the price movement in detail.
 You should not enter directly when the next candle starts forming after bullish engulfing.  You need to wait for the price to reach a fair value gap order block or support level on the lower time frame.  If the price reaches these key zones and a reversal candlestick pattern forms there, you can take a buy side trade from there.
   A bullish engulfing pattern means that the market is currently under the control of bears. Therefore this pattern is considered a strong bullish signal.  And if this bullish engulfing pattern is formed at the support level of a reaction zone such as the demand zone , then the success probability of this trade increases even more.
  Similarly, the Bearish Engulfing pattern is a formation of two candles.  The first candle is a small bullish one and the second candle is a strong bearish one that completely engulfs the body of the first candle. If we understand the psychology of this candlestick, then when the first green candle is formed then there is more buying pressure in the market.
This results in the formation of a small bullish candle. But when the next session starts, the bears again try to push the price up. During this time, the price reaches the high of the first candle and the liquidity there disappears.  After that the sellers become active from there and start aggressive selling.
  Due to this selling pressure, the price also breaks the low of the first green candle.  After which the liquidity of the low of the green candle also disappears. In the end, the price closes as a strong red candle that engulfs the entire green candle.  In this way, liquidity is lost on both the upside and downside. This is why I have categorized this candlestick formation as a dual trap pattern.
How to trade it ?  Let's understand this. To trade the bearish engulfing pattern, you don't have to use only lower time frame charts.   This tells us that the liquidity below the low of the red candle has been swept away and now it is the  turn of the liquidity sweep to the high of the previous red candle.  Price has made a swing high here and a swing low here. Now watch as the price moves up and breaks its swing high. [Music] Let's first understand what market structure is. When we look at price charts, we understand that the market doesn't move randomly.
  It does so independently, rather than following a structure. This structure is what we call market structure. There  are different theories to understand market structure, but we will try to cover the most important theory in this episode. Our first theory is Dow Theory. According to Dow Theory, price can move in only three ways: uptrend, downtrend, and sideways.
 Let's understand these in a simple and logical way that is useful to a trader. When the market is consistently making higher highs and higher lows, we call it an uptrend, meaning the market is gradually moving in an uptrend. At this time, demand exceeds supply, and buyers are interested in buying at higher prices,  while sellers are not strong enough to drive the price down.
 When the market is in an uptrend, the price moves in two phases. The first is an impulsive move, in which buyers aggressively push the price upward, creating a strong upward move. The second is a retracement move, which is a temporary pullback when traders lock in their profits.  They book, and after a retracement, the market is ready to move higher again.
 A downtrend market is the exact opposite of an uptrend. When the price consistently makes lower highs and lower lows, it is called a downtrend. In this situation, sellers are more powerful than buyers and try to push the market down. In a downtrend, the price moves in two ways: the first is an impulsive move, in which sellers aggressively push the price downward,  creating a strong downward movement in the market.
 This is followed by a retracement move, which is a temporary pullback due to profit-booking by sellers. When this pullback is complete, the market begins to fall again, and the downtrend continues. The place where most traders make mistakes is a sideways market. This is a phase in which the price moves between flat highs and flat lows.  This means that buyers and sellers are equally powerful.
 It is a waiting phase in which the market gathers its energy for a major move. We can think of it as a tug-of-war.  In which sometimes buyers push the price up, and sometimes sellers push it down, but neither of them wins. The biggest mistake traders make in a sideways market is to rush after a breakout. The  best way to trade a sideways market is to wait a bit and see who, buyers or sellers, is taking control of the market.
After a sideways market, do n't plan a sideways trade until a clear trend is visible. So far, we've understood the three main market movements: uptrend, downtrend, and sideways market.  Therefore, the first step is to understand whether the market is in an uptrend, downtrend, or sideways. Whichever direction the market is moving, we should focus only on trades in that direction. If the market is in an uptrend, look for buying opportunities, and if it is in a downtrend, focus on selling opportunities.
  We need to properly identify the market structure,  as it indicates whether we should buy or sell. If the market is in an uptrend, we need to see whether the price is in an impulsive move or a retracement. In an impulsive move, if the price is already moving at a higher speed, buying immediately can be risky. However, if the price is in a retracement move, a good buying entry point can be found.
Similarly, if the market is in a downtrend, the best selling opportunity is found during a retracement.  Now, let's understand how we  How to Identify the Beginning of an Impulsive Move and the End of a Retracement Move   Knowledge of support and resistance is crucial for identifying the end of a move. Support is a level where demand is so strong that when the price reaches there, it stops falling, and buyers enter and push the price upwards.
 Resistance is a level where supply is so strong that when the price reaches there, it stops rising, and sellers enter and push the price down. These levels help us identify when a move ends. If we consider this with an example, as you can see in this chart, the price is moving in an uptrend as it makes higher highs and higher lows.

 Now, we need to focus on where the retracement phase might end. During a retracement, a logical area is the former support level, which now acts as resistance.  If the market is in a downtrend, the double bottom pattern indicates that sellers are weakening and buyers are gaining strength, indicating a bullish reversal. Now, let's  explore another powerful method of market structure called the Whack-Off Method. According to the Whack-Off Method, the price of any traded asset goes through four distinct phases that reflect the intentions of smart money. When the market is moving in a downtrend, a stage comes when the price stops falling and begins to consolidate in a range, which we call the acumen phase.
 During this time, big players and smart money quietly begin building their positions. These big players don't directly place large buy orders, but instead use false breakouts or manipulation to create liquidity in the market. They drive the price below the support of the range and lure retail traders. When retail traders witness a breakdown and engage in panic selling, large players get an opportunity to buy at cheaper prices.
Institutions place large buy orders as soon as they complete their acumen and manipulation. The market shifts to an uptrend with a strong breakout, and the markup phase begins. In this phase, the price begins to make higher highs and higher lows, confirming that buyers have taken control of the market. After the breakout, new buyers, including retail traders, are attracted to the market.
When the price, following the markup phase,  reaches a point where the price stops moving upward and begins to consolidate within a range, this is called the distribution phase. During this period, institutions and smart money gradually begin to sell their holdings so they can exit at a higher price.
 Large players do not sell all their holdings at once because if they sell all at once, the price will immediately fall.  And their remaining holdings will be lost. Therefore, first, large players break out of the resistance level of this distribution phase. When this breakout occurs, retail traders assume the uptrend is resuming and begin buying.
 When retail traders buy, large players gain liquidity for selling and sell their holdings. After this, the distribution phase breaks down, trapping retail buyers, and a market down phase begins. In this phase, the price moves downward, forming lower highs and lower lows,  indicating that sellers have now taken control of the market.
 Now, let's understand this with a real chart example and how to trade market structure using the Wedge Method. As you can see here, the market is moving sideways, which indicates that buyers are exiting their positions here.  Before the price breaks the support level, it breaks down, which is a false breakdown.
 Its purpose is to hit stop losses and trap sellers.  This is because sellers believe the market will now enter a downtrend. As soon as sellers short, the market returns to its range, signaling that large players are absorbing liquidity. Before the actual move, the price explodes sharply upwards immediately afterward, confirming that the next phase has begun.
Now, we wait for a buying setup to enter the markup phase. At this time, buyers control the price, and they push the price aggressively. However, no trend lasts forever. There comes a time when buyers no longer push the price higher because they begin selling their positions to  book profits.
 This is why the distribution phase begins. In this phase, buyers don't sell all their holdings at once, because doing so would cause the price to fall immediately, potentially reducing their profits. Therefore, they first break out the resistance level to attract new buyers. As soon as the price breaks the resistance level, new buyers start buying, but this creates liquidity for the large players.
  From here, big players start selling their holdings, and the price returns to the range, signaling that the uptrend is weakening. When the price breaks support, a mark-down phase begins, in which the price falls, forming lower highs and lower lows. In this phase, we should focus only on short trades. In this way, using the wave-of-moments method, you can understand the market cycle: whether the market is in the acumen phase, the manipulation phase, the mark-up phase, the distribution phase, or the mark-down phase. avoiding traps and taking high-probability trades becomes easier. This is only possible if you practice and apply it to real charts.   A breakout indicates that major players have entered the market. When major players enter the market, they don't allow the market to move in the opposite direction of their entry level.  Therefore, when the price reaches a resistance or support level again, the probability of rejection is high, as major players rebuild their positions at these levels.
Therefore, you should observe when the price returns to a significant support or resistance level  after directly breaking out with a strong candle and returns to that level. If we receive a rejection signal, we can plan a long trade from there. We should place our stop-loss above the support level and set our target to the nearest swing high.
After this, you will notice that the price begins to move in your trade direction from here, as major players also rebuild their positions from such levels. Therefore, whenever the price directly breaks out of a support or resistance level with a strong candle without any retracement, you should wait for the price to return to this level.
  If you see a rejection signal there, you can plan your trade from there. [Music] Let's first understand what a trend line is. A trend line is a simple line that we draw on a chart to understand the market trend. When the market is in a downtrend, the price moves in waves. First, there's a run, then a pullback, and then another run. In a downtrend, the price continuously falls, with each new high below the previous high and each new low below the previous low.
 To  draw a trend line in a downtrend, we connect the highest swing highs. This trend line lies above the price and acts as dynamic resistance. When the price reaches this trend line, there's a  greater chance of the price falling again. Because during a downtrend, the trend line acts as resistance. If the market is in an uptrend, the price also moves in waves.

 When the price reaches this trend line, there's a  higher chance of the price moving higher again. During an uptrend, the trend line acts as a support level. When the market is in an uptrend, the trend line acts as support.

 In an uptrend, the trend line is below the price. If the price breaks below this line, it  could signal the start of a downtrend.  In this situation, selling pressure exceeds that of buyers, and the probability of further price decline is high. If you trade without identifying reaction zones, the market could  confuse you with fake breakouts and unexpected reversals.
 Therefore, for a strong trade setup, carefully understand and utilize reaction zones.  It's important to use them correctly. The first reaction zone is support and resistance levels. Support levels become more important when the market is in an uptrend. In an uptrend, the price continuously makes higher highs and higher lows.
 When the price temporarily falls below support levels, buyers push the price upwards from there, continuing the market's bullish trend. When the price temporarily dips, or pulls back, and when the market is in a downtrend, resistance levels become more critical.
In a downtrend, the price is making lower lows and lower highs. When the price temporarily pulls back and reaches resistance levels, sellers push the price downwards from there, continuing the market's bearish trend. Therefore, in a downtrend, it's important to identify resistance levels where you can find selling opportunities.
  This can happen when the price is temporarily rising, or pulling back. The second reaction zone is the demand and supply zone, also known as the order block. The demand zone refers to a level in an uptrend where buyers have strong control. When the market is in an uptrend, the price is making higher highs and higher lows.
If the price temporarily falls and reaches a demand zone, this could be a strong buying opportunity. The demand zone is an area where buyers previously pushed the price upward. When the price returns to that zone, buyers become active again and push the price upward again. Therefore, when the price reaches the demand zone, there is a possibility of a price reversal or bounce.
This level acts as strong support in the market, where the price retraces, and it is best to trade with the trend from here. Similarly, the supply zone refers to a level in a downtrend where sellers have strong control. When the market is in a downtrend,  Price is forming lower highs and lower lows. If the price temporarily pulls back and reaches a supply zone, it could be a strong selling opportunity.
 This is an area where sellers previously pushed the price down. When the price returns to this zone, sellers become active again and push the price down. Therefore, when the price reaches the supply zone, there is a possibility of a price reversal or fall. This level acts as a strong resistance in the market, where the price retraces, and it is  best to trade with the trend from here.
 The third reaction zone is the fair value gap. The concept of a fair value gap  refers to the displacement of price in the market where, during a strong move, the price creates a gap in an area where there was no price action in the previous candles. These gaps indicate inefficiency in the market. When the price returns to fill these gaps, a price reversal may occur.
  When the market is in an uptrend and the price creates a fair value gap during a strong bullish move, the price returns to fill the gap. This level acts as support. When the price returns to the fair value gap during a downtrend, buyers become active there, as this is the same level where they previously pushed the price upward.
 Therefore, the fair value gap acts as a demand zone in a downtrend, where the price has a high probability of bouncing. As soon as the price mitigates the gap, it begins to move upward and continues with the trend. Similarly, when the market is in a downtrend and the price creates a fair value gap during a strong bearish move, the price returns to fill the gap.
In a downtrend, when the price returns to the fair value gap, this level acts as resistance, as sellers return there, pushing the price downward. Therefore, the fair value gap acts as a supply zone in a downtrend, where the price is likely to be rejected.  There is a probability of , and the price starts moving downwards.

Similarly, in a down trend, when the market is moving downwards, the trend line acts as resistance.
 When the price reaches the trend line, sellers push the price downwards from there, and the market's bearish trend continues. If you are selling from a resistance level, your stop loss should be placed above the resistance level.  If the higher low point is far away, you can place your stop loss below the reaction zone from where you are planning a buying trade. If the lower high point is far away, you can place your stop loss above the reaction zone from where you are planning a selling trade.

A liquidity sweep is a trading phenomenon used in the smart money concept. It means that major market players, such as banks or institutions, deliberately force prices to a specific level.
